\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

For any
M,T_{0},T>0
, there exists a coupling of

\{u_{M}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\{u^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\mathbb{E}(u_{M}^{t}-u^{t})^{2}\leq\varepsilon(M)+\sqrt{T}\,\varepsilon(T_{0}),

where
\varepsilon(M)
does not depend on
T_{0},T
and

\varepsilon(T_{0})
does not depend on
M,T
, and

\lim_{M\to\infty}\varepsilon(M)=0
and
\lim_{T_{0}\to\infty}\varepsilon(T_{0})=0
.

Proof.

Define the covariance kernel
C_{\eta}^{(\infty)}(t,s)=\int_{\iota}^{\infty}\big(e^{-a|t-s|}-e^{-a(t+s)}\big)\mu_{\eta}(\mathrm{d}a)+c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)

representing the
M\to\infty
limit of (
62
). We
will couple Gaussian processes with covariance kernels
(C_{\eta}^{(M)},C_{\eta}^{(\infty)})
and with
(C_{\eta}^{(\infty)},C_{\eta})
respectively.

Coupling of
(C_{\eta}^{(M)},C_{\eta}^{(\infty)})
.
Let
M^{\prime}>M
be
any positive integer for which
\sqrt{M^{\prime}}
is an integer multiple of

\sqrt{M}
, and let
\{\tilde{a}_{m}\}_{m=0}^{M^{\prime}}
and

\{\tilde{c}_{m}\}_{m=1}^{M^{\prime}}
be the sequences as defined above with
M^{\prime}
in
place of
M
. Note then that the grid points
\{a_{j}\}_{j=0}^{M}

are a subset of the grid points
\{\tilde{a}_{i}\}_{i=0}^{M^{\prime}}
. Let

\displaystyle u_{M^{\prime}}^{t}=z+\sum_{i=1}^{M^{\prime}}\tilde{c}_{i}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\tilde{a}_{i}(t-s)}\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{b}^{s}_{i}

(65)

where
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))
and
\{\tilde{b}^{t}_{1}\}_{t\geq 0},\ldots\{\tilde{b}^{t}_{M^{\prime}}\}_{t\geq 0}
are standard Brownian motions independent
of each other and of
z
. Then (
62
) shows that

\{u_{M^{\prime}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
has covariance
C_{\eta}^{(M^{\prime})}
.
Now, for each
j=1,\ldots,M
, let

I_{j}=\{i:a_{j-1}<\tilde{a}_{i}\leq a_{j}\},\qquad b^{t}_{j}=\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{b}^{t}_{i}\bigg/\sqrt{\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{i}^{2}}

and set

\displaystyle u_{M}^{t}=z+\sum_{j=1}^{M}c_{j}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-a_{j}(t-s)}\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b^{s}_{j}.

Then
\{b^{t}_{1}\}_{t\geq 0},\ldots,\{b^{t}_{M}\}_{t\geq 0}
are standard
Brownian motions independent of each other and of
z
, so
(
62
) shows also that

\{u^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is a Gaussian process with covariance
C_{\eta}^{(M)}
.

We may now bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(u_{M}^{t}-u_{M^{\prime}}^{t})^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq 4\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{i:\tilde{a}_{i}>a_{M}}\tilde{c}_{i}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\tilde{a}_{i}(t-s)}\mathrm{d}\tilde{b}^{s}_{i}\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+4\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{M}\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{i}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\tilde{a}_{i}(t-s)}\mathrm{d}\tilde{b}^{i}_{s}-\sum_{j=1}^{M}c_{j}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-a_{j}(t-s)}\mathrm{d}b^{s}_{j}\Big)^{2}\Big].

Since
a_{M}=\iota+\sqrt{M}
, the first term equals
\sum_{i:\tilde{a}_{i}>\iota+\sqrt{M}}\tilde{c}_{i}^{2}/\tilde{a}_{i}=\sum_{i:\tilde{a}_{i}>\iota+\sqrt{M}}\mu_{\eta}([\tilde{a}_{i-1},\tilde{a}_{i}))
,
which is at most some
\varepsilon_{1}(M)
satisfying
\lim_{M\to\infty}\varepsilon_{1}(M)=0
, by finiteness of the measure
\mu_{\eta}
. The second term is
bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{M}\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{i}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\tilde{a}_{i}(t-s)}\mathrm{d}\tilde{b}^{s}_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{M}c_{j}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-a_{j}(t-s)}\mathrm{d}b^{s}_{j}\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{M}\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\tilde{c}_{i}e^{-\tilde{a}_{i}(t-s)}-\frac{c_{j}\tilde{c}_{i}}{\sqrt{\sum_{\ell\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{\ell}^{2}}}e^{-a_{j}(t-s)}\Big)\mathrm{d}\tilde{b}^{s}_{i}\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\tilde{c}_{i}e^{-\tilde{a}_{i}s}-\frac{c_{j}\tilde{c}_{i}}{\sqrt{\sum_{\ell\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{\ell}^{2}}}e^{-a_{j}s}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq 2(\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}),

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{c}_{i}^{2}\big(e^{-\tilde{a}_{i}s}-e^{-a_{j}s}\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}s,\qquad\mathrm{II}=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{c}_{i}^{2}\Big(1-\frac{c_{j}}{\sqrt{\sum_{\ell\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{\ell}^{2}}}\Big)^{2}e^{-2a_{j}s}\mathrm{d}s.

Let
\Delta=1/\sqrt{M}
be the spacing of
\{a_{j}\}_{j=0}^{M}
. Then,
since
|\tilde{a}_{i}-a_{j}|\leq\Delta
and
\tilde{a}_{i}\leq a_{j}

for all
i\in I_{j}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{M}\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{c}_{i}^{2}e^{-2a_{j-1}s}s^{2}\Delta^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\sum_{j=1}^{M}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\frac{\tilde{c}_{i}^{2}}{\tilde{a}_{i}}\Big)a_{j}e^{-2a_{j-1}s}s^{2}\Delta^{2}\mathrm{d}s\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{=}}\sum_{j=1}^{M}c_{j}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2a_{j-1}s}s^{2}\Delta^{2}\mathrm{d}s

where we use
\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{i}^{2}/\tilde{a}_{i}=\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\mu_{\eta}([\tilde{a}_{i-1},\tilde{a}_{i}))=\mu_{\eta}([a_{j-1},a_{j}))=c_{j}^{2}/a_{j}
in
(\ast)
. Evaluating this integral, for
an absolute constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\frac{c_{j}^{2}}{a_{j-1}^{3}}\leq\frac{C\Delta^{2}}{\iota^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\frac{c_{j}^{2}}{a_{j}}\leq\frac{C\Delta^{2}}{\iota^{2}}\mu_{\eta}([\iota,\infty))\leq\varepsilon_{2}(M)

where
\lim_{M\to\infty}\varepsilon_{2}(M)=0
. For
\mathrm{II}
,
since
c_{j}^{2}=\sum_{\ell\in I_{j}}\frac{a_{j}}{\tilde{a}_{\ell}}\tilde{c}_{\ell}^{2}
, we have
|c_{j}^{2}-\sum_{\ell\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{\ell}^{2}|\leq\Delta\sum_{\ell\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{\ell}^{2}/\tilde{a}_{\ell}=\Delta c_{j}^{2}/a_{j}
, and hence

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{M}\sum_{i\in I_{j}}\frac{\tilde{c}_{i}^{2}}{2a_{j}}\frac{(c_{j}-\sqrt{\sum_{\ell\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{\ell}^{2}})^{2}}{\sum_{\ell\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{\ell}^{2}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{M}\frac{(c_{j}^{2}-\sum_{\ell\in I_{j}}\tilde{c}_{\ell}^{2})^{2}}{2a_{j}c_{j}^{2}}\leq\frac{\Delta^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\frac{c_{j}^{2}}{a_{j}^{3}}\leq\frac{\Delta^{2}}{2\iota^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\frac{c_{j}^{2}}{a_{j}}\leq\varepsilon_{3}(M)

where
\lim_{M\to\infty}\varepsilon_{3}(M)=0
.
In summary, we have shown that
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}[(u_{M}^{t}-u_{M^{\prime}}^{t})^{2}]\leq\varepsilon(M)
for some
\varepsilon(M)\rightarrow 0
as
M\rightarrow\infty
.

Now note that for any fixed
T_{0}
and
T
,

\{u_{M^{\prime}}^{t}\}_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}
has covariance kernel

C_{\eta}^{(M^{\prime})}
that converges uniformly to
C_{\eta}^{(\infty)}
over

[T_{0},T_{0}+T]
as
M^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty
. It is direct to check from its
definitions that
C_{\eta}^{(\infty)}
satisfies the condition
(
235
) of Lemma
D.1
.
So by Lemma
D.1
, there exists a
coupling of
\{u_{M^{\prime}}^{t}\}_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}
and a Gaussian process

\{u_{\infty}^{t}\}_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}
with covariance
\{C_{\eta}^{(\infty)}(t,s)\}_{s,t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}

such that
\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\mathbb{E}(u_{M^{\prime}}^{t}-u_{\infty}^{t})^{2}\rightarrow 0

as
M^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty
. Combining this with the above
bound
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}[(u_{M}^{t}-u_{M^{\prime}}^{t})^{2}]\leq\varepsilon(M)
and taking
M^{\prime}\to\infty
shows

\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\mathbb{E}(u_{M}^{t}-u_{\infty}^{t})^{2}\leq\varepsilon(M)
.

Coupling of
(C_{\eta}^{(\infty)},C_{\eta})
.
By the approximation
(
31
) for
C_{\eta}
in Definition
2.4
,
we have for any
t\geq s\geq 0
that

|C_{\eta}(t,s)-C_{\eta}^{(\infty)}(t,s)|\leq\varepsilon(s)+\int_{\iota}^{\infty}e^{-a(t+s)}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\eta}(a),

so there exists a (different) function
\varepsilon(T_{0})
with
\lim_{T_{0}\to\infty}\varepsilon(T_{0})=0
such that

\sup_{s,t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}|C_{\eta}(t,s)-C_{\eta}^{(\infty)}(t,s)|\leq\varepsilon(T_{0}).

Here
C_{\eta}^{(\infty)}

satisfies (
235
) for a constant
C_{0}>0

depending only on
\mu_{\eta}
,
so by Lemma
D.1
, there exists a coupling
of
\{u_{\infty}^{t}\}_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}
with
\{u^{t}\}_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}
,
the latter having covariance
\{C_{\eta}(t,s)\}_{s,t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}
, for which

\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\mathbb{E}(u_{\infty}^{t}-u^{t})^{2}\leq C(\sqrt{T\,\varepsilon(T_{0})}+\varepsilon(T_{0}))

for a constant
C>0
.

Combining these two couplings yields a coupling
of
\{u_{M}^{t}\}_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}
with
\{u^{t}\}_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}
such that

\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\mathbb{E}(u_{M}^{t}-u^{t})^{2}\leq\varepsilon(M)+C(\sqrt{T\,\varepsilon(T_{0})}+\varepsilon(T_{0}))
,
and extending this arbitrarily to a full coupling of
\{u_{M}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

and
\{u^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and adjusting the value of
\varepsilon(T_{0})
shows the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.3
.

Suppose
c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)<\delta/\sigma^{2}
. Then
for any
M,T_{0},T>0
, there exists a coupling of
the processes
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\{\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

defined by (
63
) and (
64
) such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}+T]}\mathbb{E}|\theta^{t}-\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t}|\leq\varepsilon(M)+\sqrt{T}\,\varepsilon(T_{0}),

where
\varepsilon(M)
does not depend on
T_{0},T
and

\varepsilon(T_{0})
does not depend on
M,T
, and

\lim_{M\to\infty}\varepsilon(M)=0
and
\lim_{T_{0}\to\infty}\varepsilon(T_{0})=0
.

Proof.

To ease notation, let us write
\tilde{\theta}^{t}=\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t}
and
\tilde{u}^{t}=u_{M}^{t}
. We couple
\{u^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\{\tilde{u}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

according to Lemma
3.2
.
By definition,
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}
and

\{\tilde{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}
coincide up to time
T_{0}
.

To construct the coupling of
\theta^{t}
and
\tilde{\theta}^{t}
for times

t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]
, we adapt the ideas of

[
77
,
78
]
:
Fix some
\varepsilon>0
, and let
h:[0,\infty)\to[0,1]
be a function
such that
h(0)=0
,
h(x)>0
for
x>0
,
h(x)=1
for
x\geq\varepsilon
,
and both
x\mapsto h(x)
and
x\mapsto\sqrt{1-h(x)^{2}}
are Lipschitz. Let

\{b^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
and

\{\tilde{b}^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
be two standard Brownian motions initialized at

b^{T_{0}}=\tilde{b}^{T_{0}}=0
, independent of each other and of

\{u^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
,
\{\tilde{u}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
,
\theta^{*}
, and

\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}
. We define a coupling of

\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
and
\{\tilde{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
by
the joint evolutions, for
t\geq T_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\theta^{t}

\displaystyle=\Big[U(\theta^{t},\theta^{*})+\int_{0}^{t}R_{\eta}(t,s)(\theta^{s}-\theta^{*})\mathrm{d}s+u^{t}\Big]\mathrm{d}t+h(|\theta^{t}-\tilde{\theta}^{t}|)\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b^{t}+\sqrt{2(1-h(|\theta^{t}-\tilde{\theta}^{t}|)^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{b}^{t},

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\tilde{\theta}^{t}

\displaystyle=\Big[U(\tilde{\theta}^{t},\theta^{*})+\int_{0}^{t}R_{\eta}^{(M)}(t-s)(\tilde{\theta}^{s}-\theta^{*})\mathrm{d}s+\tilde{u}^{t}\Big]\mathrm{d}t-h(|\theta^{t}-\tilde{\theta}^{t}|)\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b^{t}+\sqrt{2(1-h(|\theta^{t}-\tilde{\theta}^{t}|)^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{b}^{t}.

Thus the coupling of the Brownian motions defining these processes
is by reflection at times
t\geq T_{0}
where
|\theta^{t}-\tilde{\theta}^{t}|\geq\varepsilon
, and it transitions to a synchronous coupling as
|\theta^{t}-\tilde{\theta}^{t}|\to 0
.
Lévy’s characterization of Brownian motion shows that the resulting
marginal laws of
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
and
\{\tilde{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
indeed coincide with those of (
63
) and
(
64
).

Let us write as shorthand

\displaystyle\xi^{t}

\displaystyle=\theta^{t}-\tilde{\theta}^{t}

\displaystyle v^{t}

\displaystyle=U(\theta^{t},\theta^{\ast})+\int_{0}^{t}R_{\eta}(t,s)(\theta^{s}-\theta^{\ast})\mathrm{d}s+u^{t}

\displaystyle\tilde{v}^{t}

\displaystyle=U(\tilde{\theta}^{t},\theta^{\ast})+\int_{0}^{t}R_{\eta}^{(M)}(t-s)(\tilde{\theta}^{s}-\theta^{\ast})\mathrm{d}s+\tilde{u}^{t}.

We derive a SDE for
|\xi^{t}|
that is analogous to
[
78
, Eq. (66)]
:
For any
t\geq T_{0}
, since
\mathrm{d}\xi^{t}=(v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})\mathrm{d}t+2\sqrt{2}h(|\xi^{t}|)\mathrm{d}b^{t}
,
Itô’s formula yields

\mathrm{d}(\xi^{t})^{2}=2\xi^{t}[(v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})\mathrm{d}t+2\sqrt{2}h(|\xi^{t}|)\mathrm{d}b^{t}]+8h(|\xi^{t}|)^{2}\mathrm{d}t.

For a small constant
\beta>0
,
let
S_{\beta}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
be a twice continuously-differentiable
approximation to the square root,
satisfying
S_{\beta}(x)=\sqrt{x}
for
x\geq\beta
,

\sup_{0\leq x\leq\beta}|S_{\beta}(x)|\leq C
,

\sup_{0\leq x\leq\beta}|S_{\beta}^{\prime}(x)|\leq C\beta^{-1/2}
,
and
\sup_{0\leq x\leq\beta}|S_{\beta}^{\prime\prime}(x)|\leq C\beta^{-3/2}

for a universal constant
C>0
. (A specific construction is given
in
[
78
, Eq. (68)]
.) Then again by Itô’s formula,
for any
t\geq T_{0}
,

\mathrm{d}S_{\beta}((\xi^{t})^{2})=S_{\beta}^{\prime}((\xi^{t})^{2})\Big[2\xi^{t}(v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})\mathrm{d}t+4\sqrt{2}\,\xi^{t}h(|\xi^{t}|)\mathrm{d}b^{t}+8h(|\xi^{t}|)^{2}\mathrm{d}t\Big]+16S_{\beta}^{\prime\prime}((\xi^{t})^{2})(\xi^{t})^{2}h(|\xi^{t}|)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t.

(66)

We may take the limit
\beta\to 0
via a dominated convergence argument:
Applying
S_{\beta}^{\prime}(x)=x^{-1/2}/2
for
x\geq\beta
and
the bound
|S_{\beta}^{\prime}(x)|\leq C\beta^{-1/2}
for
x<\beta
,
we have
|S_{\beta}^{\prime}((\xi^{t})^{2})\xi^{t}(v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})|\leq\max(C,1/2)|v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t}|
. Since
v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t}
is continuous and
hence integrable over
[T_{0},t]
, by dominated convergence

\lim_{\beta\to 0}\int_{T_{0}}^{t}S_{\beta}^{\prime}((\xi^{t})^{2})\xi^{t}(v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})\mathrm{d}t=\int_{T_{0}}^{t}\lim_{\beta\to 0}S_{\beta}^{\prime}((\xi^{t})^{2})\xi^{t}(v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})\mathrm{d}t=\int_{T_{0}}^{t}\frac{\sign(\xi^{t})}{2}(v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})\mathrm{d}t.

Applying the Lipschitz bound
h(|\xi^{t}|)\leq h(0)+C|\xi^{t}|=C|\xi^{t}|
and
a similar dominated convergence argument for the other terms of
(
66
), we obtain in the limit
\beta\to 0

that for
t\geq T_{0}
,

\mathrm{d}|\xi^{t}|=\sign(\xi^{t})(v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})\mathrm{d}t+2\sqrt{2}\sign(\xi^{t})h(|\xi^{t}|)\mathrm{d}b^{t}

which is the analogue of
[
78
, Eq. (66)]
. (There is no
term corresponding to a local time of
\xi^{t}
at 0
that would instead arise under a pure reflection coupling.)

Now let
A:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
be any continuously-differentiable
function, and let
f:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
be any
continuously-differentiable function with absolutely
continuous first derivative (for which Itô’s formula applies,
c.f.
[
79
, Theorem 71]
),
and satisfying
f^{\prime}(r)\in[0,1]
and
f^{\prime\prime}(r)\leq 0
for all
r\geq 0
. Set

r^{t}=|\xi^{t}|+\int_{0}^{t}A(t-s)|\xi^{s}|\mathrm{d}s.

Then
\mathrm{d}r^{t}=\mathrm{d}|\xi^{t}|+[A(0)|\xi^{t}|+\int_{0}^{t}A^{\prime}(t-s)|\xi^{s}|\mathrm{d}s]\,\mathrm{d}t
.
Applying Itô’s formula and taking expectations gives, for
t\geq T_{0}
,

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}f(r^{t})=\mathbb{E}\Big[f^{\prime}(r^{t})\Big(\sign(\xi^{t})(v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})+A(0)|\xi^{t}|+\int_{0}^{t}A^{\prime}(t-s)|\xi^{s}|\mathrm{d}s\Big)+4f^{\prime\prime}(r^{t})h(|\xi^{t}|)^{2}\Big].

(67)

Let us define
\kappa:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
by

\kappa(r)=\inf\Big\{\frac{{-}(\log g)^{\prime}(x)+(\log g)^{\prime}(y)}{x-y}:|x-y|=r\Big\}

(68)

so that
[{-}(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{t})+(\log g^{\prime})(\tilde{\theta}^{t})]/\xi^{t}\geq\kappa(|\xi^{t}|)
. Let us set also

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\big|R_{\eta}(t,s)-R_{\eta}^{(M)}(t-s)\big|\cdot\mathbb{E}|\theta^{s}-\theta^{*}|\Big)\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}|u^{t}-\tilde{u}^{t}|.

(69)

Then, under our assumption
f^{\prime}(r)\in[0,1]
, we have the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[f^{\prime}(r^{t})\sign(\xi^{t})(v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[f^{\prime}(r^{t})\sign(\xi^{t})\Big({-}\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}\xi^{t}-\big({-}(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{t})+(\log g)^{\prime}(\tilde{\theta}^{t})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\int_{0}^{t}R_{\eta}^{(M)}(t-s)(\theta^{s}-\tilde{\theta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\big(R_{\eta}(t,s)-R_{\eta}^{(M)}(t-s)\big)(\theta^{s}-\theta^{*})\mathrm{d}s+(u^{t}-\tilde{u}^{t})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbb{E}[f^{\prime}(r^{t})|\xi^{t}|]-\mathbb{E}[f^{\prime}(r^{t})\kappa(|\xi^{t}|)|\xi^{t}|]+\mathbb{E}\Big[f^{\prime}(r^{t})\int_{0}^{t}R_{\eta}^{(M)}(t-s)|\xi^{s}|\mathrm{d}s\Big]+\Delta_{t}.

Applying this to (
67
), for all
t\geq T_{0}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}f(r^{t})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[{-}\Big(\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}-A(0)\Big)f^{\prime}(r^{t})|\xi^{t}|-f^{\prime}(r^{t})\kappa(|\xi^{t}|)|\xi^{t}|+4f^{\prime\prime}(r^{t})h(|\xi^{t}|)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\mathbb{E}\Big[f^{\prime}(r^{t})\int_{0}^{t}\big(A^{\prime}(t-s)+R_{\eta}^{(M)}(t-s)\big)|\xi^{s}|\mathrm{d}s\Big]+\Delta_{t}.

(70)

Let us now choose the functions
A(\cdot)
and
f(\cdot)
.
For some small enough
c_{0}\in(0,\iota)
, let

A(0)=\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}-c_{0},\qquad A(\tau)=A(0)e^{-c_{0}\tau}-\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{-c_{0}(\tau-s)}\Big(\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}e^{-a_{m}s}\Big)\mathrm{d}s.

This choice of
A(\tau)
satisfies

A^{\prime}(\tau)=-c_{0}A(\tau)-\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}e^{-a_{m}\tau}
, i.e.

A^{\prime}(\tau)+R_{\eta}^{(M)}(\tau)=-c_{0}A(\tau).

(71)

We will require that
A(\tau)\geq 0
for all
\tau\geq 0
.
To check this condition, observe that explicitly evaluating the integral
defining
A(\tau)
yields

e^{c_{0}\tau}A(\tau)=A(0)-\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{c_{m}^{2}}{a_{m}-c_{0}}\Big(1-e^{-(a_{m}-c_{0})\tau}\Big)\geq\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}-c_{0}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{c_{m}^{2}}{a_{m}-c_{0}}\geq\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}-c_{0}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{c_{m}^{2}}{a_{m}}\cdot\frac{\iota}{\iota-c_{0}},

the last inequality using
a_{m}\geq\iota\geq c_{0}
. Further bounding

\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}/a_{m}=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mu_{\eta}([a_{m-1},a_{m}))\leq\mu_{\eta}([\iota,\infty))=c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
, this shows

e^{c_{0}\tau}A(\tau)\geq\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}-c_{0}-\frac{\iota}{\iota-c_{0}}(c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))
.
Then by the given assumption that

c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)<\delta/\sigma^{2}
, we obtain
A(\tau)\geq 0

for a sufficiently small choice of
c_{0}\in(0,\iota)
and all
\tau\geq 0
,
as desired. Applying (
71
) and
A(0)=\delta/\sigma^{2}-c_{0}
into
(
70
), and recalling the definition

r^{t}=|\xi^{t}|+\int_{0}^{t}A(t-s)|\xi^{s}|\mathrm{d}s
, we get for all
t\geq T_{0}
that

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}f(r^{t})\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\underbrace{{-}c_{0}f^{\prime}(r^{t})r^{t}-f^{\prime}(r^{t})\kappa(|\xi^{t}|)|\xi^{t}|+4f^{\prime\prime}(r^{t})h(|\xi^{t}|)^{2}}_{:=F(r^{t},\xi^{t})}\Big]+\Delta_{t}.

(72)

We next proceed to bound the above quantity
\mathbb{E}[F(r^{t},\xi^{t})]
.
Observe that under the convexity-at-infinity condition for
{-}\log g(\theta)

in Assumption
2.2
(a), there must exist
constants
R_{0},\kappa_{0}>0
for which

\kappa(r)\geq{-}\kappa_{0}\text{ for all }r\geq 0,\qquad\kappa(r)\geq 0\text{ for all }r\geq R_{0}.

(73)

Let us denote
\kappa_{-}(r)=\max(-\kappa(r),0)
.
Then
{-}\kappa(r)r\leq\kappa_{-}(r)r
and

\kappa_{-}(r)r\in[0,\kappa_{0}R_{0}]
for all
r\geq 0
.
Recall the constant
\varepsilon>0
for which
h(x)=1
when
x\geq\varepsilon
,
and define
K:(\varepsilon,\infty)\to[0,\kappa_{0}R_{0}]
by

K(r)=\sup_{t\geq T_{0}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\kappa_{-}(|\xi^{t}|)|\xi^{t}|\;\Big|\;r^{t}=r,\,|\xi^{t}|>\varepsilon\Big].

Then define
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by

f(0)=0,\qquad f^{\prime}(r)=\exp\Big({-}\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{\max(r,2\kappa_{0}R_{0}/c_{0})}K(s)ds\Big)\text{ for }r\geq 0.

Note that
f^{\prime}(r)
is absolutely continuous as required, with

f^{\prime}(r)\in[c_{1},1]
for all
r\geq 0
where

c_{1}=\exp({-}\frac{\kappa_{0}^{2}R_{0}^{2}}{2c_{0}})
, and

f^{\prime\prime}(r)={-}\frac{1}{4}K(r)f^{\prime}(r)\mathbf{1}\{r<2\kappa_{0}R_{0}/c_{0}\}\leq 0
.
By these definitions, for any
r>\varepsilon
and
t\geq T_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[F(r^{t},\xi^{t})\mid r^{t}=r,|\xi^{t}|>\varepsilon]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[{-}c_{0}f^{\prime}(r^{t})r^{t}+f^{\prime}(r^{t})\kappa_{-}(|\xi^{t}|)|\xi^{t}|+4f^{\prime\prime}(r^{t})h(|\xi^{t}|)^{2}\;\Big|\;r^{t}=r,|\xi^{t}|>\varepsilon\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[{-}c_{0}f^{\prime}(r)r+f^{\prime}(r)K(r)+4f^{\prime\prime}(r)\;\Big|\;r^{t}=r,|\xi^{t}|>\varepsilon\Big].

When
r\geq 2\kappa_{0}R_{0}/c_{0}
we may apply
f^{\prime\prime}(r)=0

and
K(r)\leq\kappa_{0}R_{0}\leq c_{0}r/2
, to bound this above by
-(c_{0}/2)f^{\prime}(r)r
.
When
r\in(\varepsilon,2\kappa_{0}R_{0}/c_{0})
we may instead apply

f^{\prime\prime}(r)={-}\frac{1}{4}K(r)f^{\prime}(r)
to see that this equals
-c_{0}f^{\prime}(r)r
.
Thus for all
r>\varepsilon
and
t\geq T_{0}
,

\mathbb{E}[F(r^{t},\xi^{t})\mid r^{t}=r,|\xi^{t}|>\varepsilon]\leq{-}(c_{0}/2)f^{\prime}(r)r.

For any
r\geq 0
, on the event
|\xi^{t}|\leq\varepsilon
(which occurs with
probability 1 when
r^{t}\leq\varepsilon
since
A(t)\geq 0
),
let us use
f^{\prime\prime}(r^{t})h(|\xi^{t}|)^{2}\leq 0
and

{-}f^{\prime}(r^{t})\kappa(|\xi^{t}|)|\xi^{t}|\leq\varepsilon\kappa_{0}
to bound

\mathbb{E}[F(r^{t},\xi^{t})\mid r^{t}=r,|\xi^{t}|\leq\varepsilon]\leq{-}c_{0}f^{\prime}(r)r+\varepsilon\kappa_{0}.

Combining these cases and taking the full expectation over

\mathbf{1}\{|\xi^{t}|>\varepsilon\}
and over
r^{t}
, we get for all
t\geq T_{0}
that

\mathbb{E}[F(r^{t},\xi^{t})]\leq{-}(c_{0}/2)\mathbb{E}[f^{\prime}(r^{t})r^{t}]+\varepsilon\kappa_{0}.

Applying
f^{\prime}(r^{t})\geq c_{1}
and
r^{t}\geq f(r^{t})
and putting this bound into
(
72
), for all
t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]
,

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}f(r^{t})\leq{-}(c_{0}c_{1}/2)\mathbb{E}f(r^{t})+\varepsilon\kappa_{0}+\max_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\Delta_{t}.

Since
f(r^{T_{0}})=f(0)=0
, this differential inequality yields for
all
t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]
,

\mathbb{E}f(r^{t})\leq\Big(\varepsilon\kappa_{0}+\max_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\Delta_{t}\Big)\frac{1-e^{-(c_{0}c_{1}/2)(t-T_{0})}}{c_{0}c_{1}/2}\leq\frac{2}{c_{0}c_{1}}\Big(\varepsilon\kappa_{0}+\max_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\Delta_{t}\Big).

Since also
r^{t}\leq c_{1}f(r^{t})
from the lower bound
f^{\prime}(r)\geq c_{1}
, this
gives
\mathbb{E}r^{t}\leq(2/c_{0})(\varepsilon\kappa_{0}+\max_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\Delta_{t})
.
Applying that
A(t)\geq 0
for all
t\geq 0
, we have

|\xi^{t}|\leq r^{t}
, so this gives finally

\max_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\mathbb{E}|\theta^{t}-\tilde{\theta}^{t}|=\max_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\mathbb{E}|\xi^{t}|\leq(2/c_{0})\Big(\varepsilon\kappa_{0}+\max_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\Delta_{t}\Big).

We may choose
\varepsilon
such that

\varepsilon\kappa_{0}\leq\max_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\Delta_{t}
. Thus,
to conclude the proof, it remains to show

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\Delta_{t}\leq\varepsilon(M)+\sqrt{T}\,\varepsilon(T_{0}).

(74)

We note that under Definition
2.4
,

\mathbb{E}|\theta^{t}-\theta^{\ast}|\leq[\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}-\theta^{\ast})^{2}]^{1/2}=(C_{\theta}(t,t)-2C_{\theta}(t,*)+\mathbb{E}{\theta^{*}}^{2})^{1/2}\leq C

for a constant
C>0
and all
t\geq 0
. Then for the first term of
\Delta_{t}
,
by property (
35
) of Definition
2.4
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|R_{\eta}(t,s)-R_{\eta}^{(M)}(t-s)|\cdot\mathbb{E}|\theta^{s}-\theta^{\ast}|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}|R_{\eta}(t,s)-r^{\mathrm{tti}}_{\eta}(t-s)|\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{0}^{t}|r_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(t-s)-R_{\eta}^{(M)}(t-s)|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon(t)+\int_{0}^{t}|r_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(t-s)-R_{\eta}^{(M)}(t-s)|\mathrm{d}s

where here
\lim_{t\to\infty}\varepsilon(t)=0
.
Recalling the sequences
\{a_{m}\}_{m=0}^{M},\{c_{m}\}_{m=1}^{M}
defining

R_{\eta}^{(M)}
,

\displaystyle r_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)-R_{\eta}^{(M)}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\int_{\iota}^{\infty}ae^{-a\tau}\mu_{\eta}(\mathrm{d}a)-\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}e^{-a_{m}\tau}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\int_{a_{m-1}}^{a_{m}}(ae^{-a\tau}-a_{m}e^{-a_{m}\tau})\mu_{\eta}(\mathrm{d}a)+\int_{a:a>a_{M}}ae^{-a\tau}\mu_{\eta}(\mathrm{d}a),

hence using the fact that
h(a)=ae^{-a\tau}
satisfies
|h^{\prime}(a)|\leq 2e^{-a\tau/2}
and
|a_{m}-a_{m-1}|=1/\sqrt{M}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\big|r_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)-R_{\eta}^{(M)}(\tau)\big|\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}\int_{a_{m-1}}^{a_{m}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|ae^{-a\tau}-a_{m}e^{-a_{m}\tau}|\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\mu_{\eta}(\mathrm{d}a)+\int_{a:a>a_{M}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}ae^{-a\tau}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\mu_{\eta}(\mathrm{d}a)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}\int_{a_{m-1}}^{a_{m}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}(2/\sqrt{M})e^{-a_{m-1}\tau/2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\mu_{\eta}(\mathrm{d}a)+\mu_{\eta}([a_{M},\infty))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\sqrt{M}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\int_{a_{m-1}}^{a_{m}}\frac{1}{a_{m-1}}\mu_{\eta}(\mathrm{d}a)+\mu_{\eta}([a_{M},\infty))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\iota\sqrt{M}}\mu_{\eta}([\iota,\sqrt{M}))+\mu_{\eta}([\sqrt{M},\infty))\leq\varepsilon(M),

where
\lim_{M\to\infty}\varepsilon(M)=0
. This bounds the first term of

\Delta_{t}
by
\varepsilon(M)+Ct\cdot\varepsilon(t)
. Bounding also the second term

\mathbb{E}|u^{t}-\tilde{u}^{t}|
of
\Delta_{t}
by Lemma
3.2
, we have

\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\Delta_{t}\leq\varepsilon(M)+\sqrt{T}\,\varepsilon(T_{0})+\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}Ct\cdot\varepsilon(t)

which implies (
74
) upon adjusting
\varepsilon(T_{0})
. This
completes the proof.
∎

Adapting part of the previous argument, we record here a uniform bound on

\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t})^{4}
for the solution
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
of the DMFT
equation.

Lemma 3.4
.

Suppose
c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)<\delta/\sigma^{2}
. Then

\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t})^{4}\leq C

and
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}(\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t})^{4}\leq C
for a constant
C>0
and
all
M,T_{0}>0
.

Proof.

We prove the statement for
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
.
Let
A:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
be defined by

A(0)=\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}-c_{0},\qquad A(\tau)=A(0)e^{-c_{0}\tau}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c_{0}(\tau-s)}r^{\mathrm{tti}}_{\eta}(s)\mathrm{d}s

for a small enough constant
c_{0}\in(0,\iota)
. Here, by the conditions of
Definition
2.4
,

r_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(s)={-}{c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}}^{\prime}(s)=\int_{\iota}^{\infty}ae^{-as}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\eta}(a)
,
and the same argument as in the preceding proof verifies that
\inf_{\tau\in[0,\infty)}A(\tau)
is bounded below by a positive constant
for a sufficiently small choice of
c_{0}\in(0,\iota)
and all
\tau\geq 0
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
be a smooth approximation to the absolute value,
satisfying
f(x)=|x|
for all
|x|\geq 1
, and

1+f^{\prime}(x)\cdot x\geq f(x)\geq|x|
,
|f^{\prime}(x)|\leq 1
, and
|f^{\prime\prime}(x)|\leq C

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and an absolute constant
C>0
.
Let
\bar{\theta}^{t}=\theta^{t}-\theta^{\ast}
, and set

r^{t}=f(\bar{\theta}^{t})+\int_{0}^{t}A(t-s)f(\bar{\theta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s
.
Then by the DMFT equation (
23
) and Itô’s
formula,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\bar{\theta}^{t}

\displaystyle=\Big[{-}\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}\bar{\theta}^{t}+(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{t})+\int_{0}^{t}R_{\eta}(t,s)\bar{\theta}^{s}\mathrm{d}s+u^{t}\Big]\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b^{t},

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}r^{t}

\displaystyle=f^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}^{t})\mathrm{d}\bar{\theta}^{t}+f^{\prime\prime}(\bar{\theta}^{t})\mathrm{d}t+\Big[A(0)f(\bar{\theta}^{t})+\int_{0}^{t}A^{\prime}(t-s)f(\bar{\theta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s\Big]\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}(r^{t})^{4}

\displaystyle=4(r^{t})^{3}\mathrm{d}r^{t}+12(r^{t})^{2}f^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}^{t})^{2}\mathrm{d}t.

Applying
r^{t}\geq 0
and
the bounds
f^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}^{t})\bar{\theta}^{t}\geq f(\bar{\theta}^{t})-1
,

|f^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}^{t})|\leq 1
,
|\bar{\theta}^{s}|\leq f(\bar{\theta}^{s})
, and

|f^{\prime\prime}(\bar{\theta}^{t})|\leq C
from the definition of
f(\cdot)
, this gives

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}(r^{t})^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[4(r^{t})^{3}\Bigg({-}\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}[f(\bar{\theta}^{t})-1]+f^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}^{t})(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{t})+\int_{0}^{t}|R_{\eta}(t,s)|f(\bar{\theta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s+|u^{t}|+C

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+A(0)f(\bar{\theta}^{t})+\int_{0}^{t}A^{\prime}(t-s)f(\bar{\theta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s\Bigg)+12(r^{t})^{2}\Bigg].

Then, using
A(0)=\delta/\sigma^{2}-c_{0}

and
A^{\prime}(t-s)+r_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(t-s)={-}c_{0}A(t-s)
from the definition of
A(\cdot)
,

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}(r^{t})^{4}\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[4(r^{t})^{3}\Bigg({-}c_{0}r^{t}+\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}+f^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}^{t})(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{t})+\int_{0}^{t}|R_{\eta}(t,s)-r_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(t-s)|f(\bar{\theta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s+|u^{t}|+C\Bigg)+12(r^{t})^{2}\Bigg].

When
|\bar{\theta}^{t}|\geq 1
, we must have

f^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}^{t})=\sign(\bar{\theta}^{t})=|\bar{\theta}^{t}|/(\theta^{t}-\theta^{*})
.
Recalling the function
\kappa(r)
from (
68
), let us bound in
this case

f^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}^{t})[(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{t})-(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{*})]=-|\bar{\theta}^{t}|\cdot\frac{-(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{t})+(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{*})}{\theta^{t}-\theta^{*}}\leq-\kappa(|\bar{\theta}^{t}|)|\bar{\theta}^{t}|\leq\kappa_{0}R_{0},

where
\kappa_{0}R_{0}
is the deterministic upper bound for
{-}\kappa(r)r
.
For
|\bar{\theta}^{t}|\leq 1
, let us apply instead the Lipschitz bound

|f^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}^{t})[(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{t})-(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{*})]|\leq L
where
L
is the Lipschitz constant of
(\log g)^{\prime}
under Assumption

2.2
(a).
We also apply
|R_{\eta}(t,s)-r_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(t-s)|\leq\varepsilon(t)
from Definition

2.4
, and
\int_{0}^{t}f(\bar{\theta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s\leq r^{t}/\inf_{\tau\in[0,\infty)}A(\tau)
by the definition of
r^{t}
,
where we recall that

\inf_{\tau\in[0,\infty)}A(\tau)
is bounded below by a positive constant.
Thus, for some constant
C^{\prime}>0
, this yields

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}(r^{t})^{4}\leq{-}4c_{0}\mathbb{E}(r^{t})^{4}+C^{\prime}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\varepsilon(t)(r^{t})^{4}+(r^{t})^{3}(1+|(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{*})|+|u^{t}|)+(r^{t})^{2}\Big].

Since
u^{t}
is a centered Gaussian variable,
we note that
\mathbb{E}(u^{t})^{4}=3[\mathbb{E}(u^{t})^{2}]^{2}=3C(t,t)^{2}
which
is bounded uniformly for all
t\geq 0

under Definition
2.4
. Also
\mathbb{E}[|(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{*})|^{4}]
is finite
by the Lipschitz continuity of
(\log g)^{\prime}
and finiteness of moments
of
\theta^{*}
under Assumption
2.1
. Then by Hölder’s inequality,

\mathbb{E}[(r^{t})^{3}(1+|(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{*})|+|u^{t}|)+(r^{t})^{2}]\leq C(\mathbb{E}[(r^{t})^{4}]^{3/4}+\mathbb{E}[(r^{t})^{4}]^{1/2})
for some
C>0
. Thus, for some
C,T,R>0
sufficiently large
depending on
C^{\prime},c_{0}
, the above implies

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}(r^{t})^{4}\leq C\,\mathbb{E}(r^{t})^{4},\qquad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}(r^{t})^{4}\leq{-}4c_{0}\mathbb{E}(r^{t})^{4}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}(r^{t})^{4}<0\text{ whenever }t\geq T\text{ and }\mathbb{E}(r^{t})^{4}\geq R.

This implies that
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}(r^{t})^{4}
is bounded by a constant depending
only on
C,T,R
. Then
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t})^{4}
is also bounded since

\theta^{t}=\bar{\theta}^{t}+\theta^{*}
and

|\bar{\theta}^{t}|\leq f(\bar{\theta}^{t})\leq r^{t}
.

The argument to bound
\mathbb{E}(\theta_{M,T_{0}})^{4}
is the same upon replacing

R_{\eta}(t,s)
and
u^{t}
by
R_{\eta}^{(M)}(t,s)
and
u_{M}^{t}
for
s,t\geq T_{0}
,
and we omit the details for brevity.
∎

3.1.2
Convergence of the auxiliary process

Extending the definition (
40
) of

\mathsf{P}_{g_{*},\omega_{*};g,\omega}
, let

\mathsf{P}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{*};g,\omega}^{\otimes 2}

denote the law of a triple
(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime})
where
\theta^{*},\theta

are generated according to (
41
) defining

\mathsf{P}_{g_{*},\omega_{*};g,\omega}

and
\theta^{\prime}
is a second independent copy of
\theta
drawn from the posterior
measure conditional on
y
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Suppose
c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)<\delta/\sigma^{2}
.
Fix any
M,T_{0}>0
, set
\omega^{(M)}=\delta/\sigma^{2}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}/a_{m}
and

\omega_{\ast}^{(M)}=(\omega^{(M)})^{2}/c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
,
and let
\{\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the
process (
64
). Then for any
T,T^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle W_{1}\Big(\mathsf{P}(\theta^{\ast},\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{T_{0}+T},\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}),\mathsf{P}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast}^{(M)};g,\omega^{(M)}}^{\otimes 2}\Big)\leq C\sqrt{M}(e^{-cT}+e^{-cT^{\prime}})

for some constants
C,c>0
not depending on
M,T_{0},T,T^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))
and let

\{\tilde{b}^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
and
\{b^{t}_{m}\}_{t\geq 0}
for

m=1,\ldots,M
be
M+1
standard Brownian motions. These are all independent of
each other, of
\theta^{*}
, and of
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
.
We note that the law of
\{\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

defined by (
64
) coincides with the
marginal law of
\{\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
in the joint process

\displaystyle\theta^{t}_{M,T_{0}}

\displaystyle=\theta^{t}\text{ for }t\in[0,T_{0}],

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\theta^{t}_{M,T_{0}}

\displaystyle=\Big[{-}\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}(\theta^{t}_{M,T_{0}}-\theta^{\ast})+(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t})+z+\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}x^{t}_{m}\Big]\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{b}^{t}\text{ for }t>T_{0},

(75)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}x^{t}_{m}

\displaystyle=[{-}a_{m}x^{t}_{m}+c_{m}(\theta^{t}_{M,T_{0}}-\theta^{\ast})]\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b^{t}_{m}\text{ for }1\leq m\leq M,\,t\geq 0

(76)

with initial conditions
x_{1}^{0}=\ldots=x_{M}^{0}=0
. Indeed,
given
\{\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
, the equations (
76
) for

\{x_{m}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
are linear and have the explicit solutions

x_{m}^{t}=c_{m}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-a_{m}(t-s)}(\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{s}-\theta^{*})\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-a_{m}(t-s)}\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b_{m}^{s}.

(77)

Substituting these solutions into (
75
) gives
(
64
), upon identifying

u_{M}^{t}=z+\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-a_{m}(t-s)}\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b_{m}^{s}
.
Here
\{u_{M}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is a centered Gaussian process independent of

\theta^{*}
and
\{\tilde{b}^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
, with covariance kernel exactly

C_{\eta}^{(M)}(t,s)
by (
62
). Thus the marginal law of

\{\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
coincides with the definition in
(
64
).

For
t\geq T_{0}
, let
x^{t}=(\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t},x_{1}^{t},\ldots,x_{M}^{t})

and
b^{t}=(\tilde{b}^{t},b_{1}^{t},\ldots,b_{M}^{t})
.
Conditional on
\theta^{*}
and
z
,
the evolution of
\{x^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
defined by
(
75
–
76
) is a standard (Markovian)
Langevin diffusion given by

\mathrm{d}x^{t}={-}\nabla H(x^{t}\mid\theta^{*},z)\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b^{t}

with Hamiltonian

\displaystyle H(x\mid\theta^{*},z)

\displaystyle=H(\theta,x_{1},\ldots,x_{M}\mid\theta^{*},z)

(78)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\underbrace{\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{c_{m}^{2}}{a_{m}}}_{=\omega^{(M)}}\Big)(\theta-\theta^{\ast})^{2}-\log g(\theta)-z\,\theta+\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{a_{m}}{2}\Big(\frac{c_{m}}{a_{m}}(\theta-\theta^{\ast})-x_{m}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega^{(M)}}{2}(\theta-\theta^{*}-z/\omega^{(M)})^{2}-\log g(\theta)+\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{a_{m}}{2}\Big(\frac{c_{m}}{a_{m}}(\theta-\theta^{\ast})-x_{m}\Big)^{2}+\text{const.},

(79)

for an additive constant not depending on
x=(\theta,x_{1},\ldots,x_{M})
.
Note that the given condition

c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)<\delta/\sigma^{2}
implies that

\omega^{(M)}>0
strictly.

Convergence of
\{x^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
in Wasserstein-1 to a stationary law
then follows from the results of

[
77
]
: For any
x=(\theta,x_{1},\ldots,x_{M})
and

x^{\prime}=(\theta^{\prime},x_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,x_{M}^{\prime})
, we have

(x-x^{\prime})^{\top}(\nabla H(x\mid\theta^{*},z)-\nabla H(x^{\prime}\mid\theta^{*},z))=(\theta-\theta^{\prime})({-}(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta)+(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta^{\prime}))+(x-x^{\prime})^{\top}L(x-x^{\prime})

where

L=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}&{-}c_{1}&\ldots&{-}c_{M}\\
{-}c_{1}&a_{1}&&\\
\vdots&&\ddots&\\
{-}c_{M}&&&a_{M}\end{pmatrix}.

By the positivity of the Schur complement

\omega^{(M)}=\delta/\sigma^{2}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}/a_{m}\geq\delta/\sigma^{2}-(c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))

and of
a_{m}\geq\iota
, this
matrix
L
is strictly positive-definite, with smallest eigenvalue bounded away
from 0 independently of
M
. Then, recalling the function

\kappa(r)
from (
68
),

(x-x^{\prime})^{\top}(\nabla H(x\mid\theta^{*},z)-\nabla H(x^{\prime}\mid\theta^{*},z))\geq(\theta-\theta^{\prime})^{2}\kappa(|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|)+c\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}

for a constant
c>0
. Recalling also
that
\kappa(r)
is positive for all
r>R_{0}

and some
R_{0}>0
, and considering separately the cases where
|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|\leq R_{0}
and
|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|>R_{0}
, this verifies that

\inf_{\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{2}=r}\frac{(x-x^{\prime})^{\top}(\nabla H(x\mid\theta^{*},z)-\nabla H(x^{\prime}\mid\theta^{*},z))}{\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}>c^{\prime}

for all
r>R_{0}^{\prime}
and some constants
c^{\prime},R_{0}^{\prime}>0
.
Then by
[
77
, Corollary 3]
, the Langevin diffusion

\{x^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}
has the unique stationary law

\mathsf{P}^{\infty}(x)\propto\exp(-H(x\mid\theta^{*},z)).

(80)

Let us write
x^{\infty}\sim\mathsf{P}^{\infty}
and

\langle f(x^{\infty})\rangle
for a sample and Gibbs average under this
stationary law. Let us write also
W_{1}(\cdot)
for the
Wasserstein-1 distance conditional on
\theta^{*},z
, and

\mathsf{P}(x^{T_{0}+T}\mid x^{T_{0}}=x)
for the conditional
law of
x^{T_{0}+T}
given
\theta^{*},z
and the initial condition
x^{T_{0}}=x
.
Then also by
[
77
, Corollary 2 and 3]
,
there exist constants
C,c>0
such that for any
T>0
,

W_{1}(\mathsf{P}(x^{T_{0}+T}\mid x^{T_{0}}=x),\mathsf{P}^{\infty})\leq Ce^{-cT}W_{1}(\delta_{x},\mathsf{P}^{\infty})\leq Ce^{-cT}(\|x\|_{2}+\langle\|x^{\infty}\|_{2}\rangle).

Similarly, for any
T^{\prime}>0
,

W_{1}(\mathsf{P}(x^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}\mid x^{T_{0}+T}=x),\mathsf{P}^{\infty})\leq Ce^{-cT^{\prime}}(\|x\|_{2}+\langle\|x^{\infty}\|_{2}\rangle).

Combining the two conditional couplings that attain these Wasserstein-1
bounds, and taking the average over the sample path
\{x^{t}\}_{t\geq T_{0}}

(which we denote by
\langle f(x^{t})\rangle
, still conditional on
\theta^{*},z
),

W_{1}(\mathsf{P}(x^{T_{0}+T},x^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}),(\mathsf{P}^{\infty})^{\otimes 2})\leq C(e^{-cT}+e^{-cT^{\prime}})(\langle\|x^{T_{0}}\|_{2}\rangle+\langle\|x^{T_{0}+T}\|_{2}\rangle+\langle\|x^{\infty}\|_{2}\rangle)

where
(\mathsf{P}^{\infty})^{\otimes 2}
is the law of two independent samples from

\mathsf{P}^{\infty}
. The explicit form (
77
) for each
\{x_{m}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
implies that
\langle|x_{m}^{t}|\rangle\leq c_{m}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\iota(t-s)}(\langle|\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{s}|\rangle+|\theta^{*}|)\mathrm{d}s+(a_{m})^{-1/2}
, and hence

\langle\|x^{t}\|_{2}\rangle\leq C\sqrt{M}\Big(1+|\theta^{*}|+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\iota(t-s)}\langle|\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{s}|\rangle\mathrm{d}s\Big)

for a constant
C>0
. Then, taking the full expectation over
\theta^{*},z

and applying
\mathbb{E}\langle|\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{t}|\rangle\leq C
by
Lemma
3.4
, we get
\mathbb{E}\langle\|x^{t}\|_{2}\rangle\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{M}
for a constant
C^{\prime}>0
and all

t\geq 0
. Then, applying this above gives

\mathbb{E}W_{1}(\mathsf{P}(x^{T_{0}+T},x^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}),(\mathsf{P}^{\infty})^{\otimes 2})\leq C\sqrt{M}(e^{-cT}+e^{-cT^{\prime}})

(81)

for some (different) constants
C,c>0
.

Finally, note that the stationary law
\mathsf{P}^{\infty}(x)
defined by
(
80
) with Hamiltonian (
79
)
describes a joint law (conditional on
\theta^{*},z
) of
(\theta,x_{1},\ldots,x_{M})

where
x_{m}\mid\theta
is Gaussian and independent across
m=1,\ldots,M
,
and
\theta
has marginal law given exactly by

\mathsf{P}(\theta\mid y)
in the Gaussian convolution
model (
37
) with observation

y=\theta^{*}+z/\omega^{(M)}
. Here, the noise variable
z/\omega^{(M)}
is
Gaussian with variance

c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)/(\omega^{(M)})^{2}=(\omega_{*}^{(M)})^{-1}
,
so the joint law of
\theta^{*}
and the
(\theta,\theta^{\prime})
-marginals of
the conditional law
(\mathsf{P}^{\infty})^{\otimes 2}
given
(\theta^{*},z)
is precisely

\mathsf{P}_{g_{*},\omega_{*}^{(M)};g,\omega^{(M)}}^{\otimes 2}
. Then, taking the

(\theta,\theta^{\prime})
-marginals of the coupling (conditional on
\theta^{*},z
)
that attains

W_{1}(\mathsf{P}(x^{T_{0}+T},x^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}),(\mathsf{P}^{\infty})^{\otimes 2})

and combining with the identity coupling of
\theta^{*}
, we have

W_{1}(\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{T_{0}+T},\theta_{M,T_{0}}^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}),\mathsf{P}_{g_{*},\omega_{*}^{(M)};g,\omega^{(M)}}^{\otimes 2})\leq W_{1}(\mathsf{P}(x^{T_{0}+T},x^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}),(\mathsf{P}^{\infty})^{\otimes 2}).

Taking the full expectation over
\theta^{*},z
on both sides and applying the
bound (
81
) shows the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

Suppose
c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)<\delta/\sigma^{2}
. For any
M>0
,
let

\displaystyle\omega^{(M)}

\displaystyle=\delta/\sigma^{2}-\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}/a_{m},\quad\omega_{\ast}^{(M)}=(\omega^{(M)})^{2}/c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty),

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle=\delta/\sigma^{2}-(c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)),\quad\omega_{\ast}=\omega^{2}/c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty).

Then
\lim_{M\to\infty}W_{1}(\mathsf{P}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast}^{(M)};g,\omega^{(M)}}^{\otimes 2},\mathsf{P}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast};g,\omega}^{\otimes 2})=0
.

Proof.

Let
(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime})\sim\mathsf{P}_{g_{*},\omega_{*};g,\omega}^{\otimes 2}
,
i.e.
\theta,\theta^{\prime}
are two independent draws from the posterior law

\mathsf{P}_{g,\omega}(\theta\mid y)
in the scalar Gaussian convolution model
(
37
) where

y=\theta^{*}+{\omega^{*}}^{-1/2}z
and
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
Let
\langle\cdot\rangle_{g,\omega}

be average over
\theta,\theta^{\prime}
conditional on

\theta^{*},z
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be the class of 1-Lipschitz functions

f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime})
. Then, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
,

\mathbb{E}_{g_{*},\omega_{*}}\langle f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime})\rangle_{g,\omega}=\mathbb{E}\frac{\int f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime})\exp(-\frac{\omega}{2}[(\theta^{*}+{\omega^{*}}^{-1/2}z-\theta)^{2}+(\theta^{*}+{\omega^{*}}^{-1/2}z-\theta^{\prime})^{2}])g(\theta)g(\theta^{\prime})\mathrm{d}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})}{\int\exp(-\frac{\omega}{2}[(\theta^{*}+{\omega^{*}}^{-1/2}z-\theta)^{2}+(\theta^{*}+{\omega^{*}}^{-1/2}z-\theta^{\prime})^{2}])g(\theta)g(\theta^{\prime})\mathrm{d}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})}

where
\mathbb{E}
on the right side is over
\theta^{*}\sim g_{*}
and
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
Writing
\langle\cdot\rangle
for
\langle\cdot\rangle_{g,\omega}
and

\kappa_{2}
for its associated posterior covariance,
the above is continuously-differentiable in
(\omega,\omega^{*})
with

\displaystyle\partial_{\omega}\mathbb{E}\langle f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\kappa_{2}\Big(f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime}),{-}\tfrac{1}{2}[(\theta^{*}+{\omega^{*}}^{-1/2}z-\theta)^{2}+(\theta^{*}+{\omega^{*}}^{-1/2}z-\theta^{\prime})^{2}]\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\partial_{\omega^{*}}\mathbb{E}\langle f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\kappa_{2}\Big(f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime}),\tfrac{\omega z}{\omega_{*}^{3/2}}[(\theta^{*}+{\omega^{*}}^{-1/2}z-\theta)+(\theta^{*}+{\omega^{*}}^{-1/2}z-\theta^{\prime})]\Big)\Big]

By the 1-Lipschitz bound for
f
and the identity
\Var X=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[(X-X^{\prime})^{2}]
where
X^{\prime}
is an independent copy of
X
, we have

\kappa_{2}(f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime}),f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime}))\leq C(\kappa_{2}(\theta,\theta)+\kappa_{2}(\theta^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}))
for an absolute
constant
C>0
. Then, applying Cauchy-Schwarz to
\kappa_{2}(\cdot)
above,
we get that
(\omega,\omega_{*})\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{g_{*},\omega_{*}}\langle f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime})\rangle_{g,\omega}

is locally Lipschitz-continuous uniformly over
f\in\mathcal{F}
. Since

\lim_{M\to\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}/a_{m}=\mu_{\eta}([\iota,\infty))=c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
,
we have
\lim_{M\to\infty}(\omega^{(M)},\omega_{*}^{(M)})=(\omega,\omega_{*})
.
Then this local Lipschitz continuity implies as desired

\lim_{M\to\infty}W_{1}(\mathsf{P}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast}^{(M)};g,\omega^{(M)}}^{\otimes 2},\mathsf{P}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast};g,\omega}^{\otimes 2})=\lim_{M\to\infty}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\Big|\mathbb{E}_{g_{*},\omega_{*}}\langle f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime})\rangle_{g,\omega}-\mathbb{E}_{g_{*},\omega_{*}^{(M)}}\langle f(\theta^{*},\theta,\theta^{\prime})\rangle_{g,\omega^{(M)}}\Big|=0.

∎

We now complete the proof of Lemma
3.1
.

Proof of Lemma
3.1
.

By Lemmas
3.3
,
3.5
, and

3.6
, for any
M,T_{0},T,T^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle W_{1}(\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{T_{0}+T},\theta^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}),\mathsf{P}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{*};g,\omega}^{\otimes 2})\leq\varepsilon(M)+2\sqrt{T+T^{\prime}}\,\varepsilon(T_{0})+C\sqrt{M}(e^{-cT}+e^{-cT^{\prime}}).

Setting
T=T^{\prime}=t
, choosing
T_{0}\equiv T_{0}(t)
so that

\lim_{t\to\infty}T_{0}(t)=\infty
and

\lim_{t\to\infty}\sqrt{2t}\,\varepsilon(T_{0}(t))=0
, and taking

t\to\infty
followed by
M\to\infty
, this shows

\lim_{t\to\infty}W_{1}(\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{T_{0}(t)+t},\theta^{T_{0}(t)+2t}),\mathsf{P}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{*};g,\omega}^{\otimes 2})=0.

In particular, we have the weak convergence in distribution of

(\theta^{*},\theta^{T_{0}(t)+t},\theta^{T_{0}(t)+2t})
to

\mathsf{P}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{*};g,\omega}^{\otimes 2}
.
Lemma
3.4
implies that

(\theta^{*},\theta^{T_{0}(t)+t},\theta^{T_{0}(t)+2t})
is uniformly bounded in
L^{4}

and hence uniformly integrable in
L^{2}
, so this implies

\lim_{t\to\infty}W_{2}(\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{T_{0}(t)+t},\theta^{T_{0}(t)+2t}),\mathsf{P}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{*};g,\omega}^{\otimes 2})=0.

(82)

Then, under Definition
2.4
and by definition of the law

\mathsf{P}_{g_{*},\omega_{*};g,\omega}^{\otimes 2}
, we have as desired

\displaystyle c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}C_{\theta}(T_{0}(t)+t,T_{0}(t)+t)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[(\theta^{T_{0}(t)+t})^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast}}\langle\theta^{2}\rangle_{g,\omega},

\displaystyle c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}C_{\theta}(T_{0}(t)+t,T_{0}(t)+2t)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[\theta^{T_{0}(t)+t}\theta^{T_{0}(t)+2t}]=\mathbb{E}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast}}\langle\theta\rangle_{g,\omega}^{2},

\displaystyle c_{\theta}(\ast)

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}C_{\theta}(T_{0}(t)+t,*)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[\theta^{T_{0}(t)+t}\theta^{\ast}]=\mathbb{E}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast}}[\langle\theta\rangle_{g,\omega}\theta^{\ast}].

∎

3.2
Analysis of
\eta
-equation

We next derive from an analysis of the evolution
(
25
) for
\{\eta^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

a representation of

c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0),c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
in terms of

c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0),c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty),c_{\theta}(*)
.

Lemma 3.7
.

It holds that

\displaystyle c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{4}}\bigg[\frac{\mathbb{E}{\theta^{*}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}+c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)-2c_{\theta}(\ast)}{\big(1+\sigma^{-2}(c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))\big)^{2}}+\frac{c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)}{1+\sigma^{-2}(c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))}\bigg],

(83)

\displaystyle c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{4}}\frac{\mathbb{E}{\theta^{*}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}+c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)-2c_{\theta}(\ast)}{(1+\sigma^{-2}(c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)))^{2}},

(84)

and in particular
c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)<\delta/\sigma^{2}
.

The argument is similar to the analysis of
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
, where we
may approximate the dynamics of
\{\eta^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
at large times by a
Markovian joint evolution of a system
(\eta^{t},x_{1}^{t},\ldots,x_{M}^{t})
. Our
argument here is simpler than before,
as the dynamics of
(\eta^{t},x_{1}^{t},\ldots,x_{M}^{t})
will
be linear, from which we may explicitly analyze the convergence of
\eta^{t}

and show that it is independent of
M
; thus we will apply a simple Gronwall
argument to bound the propagation of the discretization error
\varepsilon(M)
over
time.

3.2.1
Comparison with an auxiliary process

We again fix a positive integer
M
, and define

\{a_{m}\}_{m=0}^{M}
and
\{c_{m}\}_{m=1}^{M}
by

a_{m}=\iota+\frac{m}{\sqrt{M}}\text{ for }m=0,\ldots,M,\qquad\frac{c_{m}^{2}}{a_{m}}=\mu_{\theta}([a_{m-1},a_{m}))

with
\mu_{\theta}
now instead of
\mu_{\eta}
.
For convenience, let us introduce
\xi^{t}=\eta^{t}+w^{\ast}-\varepsilon

and
v^{t}={-}w^{t}+w^{*}-\varepsilon
, so the
DMFT equation (
25
) for
\{\eta^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is
equivalently

\xi^{t}=-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}R_{\theta}(t,s)\xi^{s}\mathrm{d}s+v^{t}.

(85)

Here,
\{v^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is a centered Gaussian process with covariance

\mathbb{E}[v^{t}v^{s}]=C_{\theta}(t,s)-C_{\theta}(t,*)-C_{\theta}(s,*)+\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}+\sigma^{2}
. We set

R_{\theta}^{(M)}(\tau)=\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}e^{-a_{m}\tau},\qquad C_{\theta}^{(M)}(t,s)=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{c_{m}^{2}}{a_{m}}(e^{-a_{m}|t-s|}-e^{-a_{m}(t+s)})+c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)

and define an auxiliary process
\{\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
by

\displaystyle\xi^{t}_{M,T_{0}}

\displaystyle=\xi^{t}\text{ for }t\in[0,T_{0})

\displaystyle\xi^{t}_{M,T_{0}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}R_{\theta}^{(M)}(t-s)\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{s}\mathrm{d}s+v_{M}^{t}\text{ for }t\geq T_{0}

(86)

where
\{v_{M}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is a centered Gaussian process with covariance

\mathbb{E}[v_{M}^{t}v_{M}^{s}]=C_{\theta}^{(M)}(t,s)-2c_{\theta}(*)+\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}+\sigma^{2}
,
defined in the probability space of
\{\xi^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
.
(We check in the proof of Lemma
3.10
below that this is
indeed a positive-semidefinite covariance kernel.) We note that the process

\{\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
may be discontinuous at
T_{0}
; this is
inconsequential for our subsequent analysis.

Lemma 3.8
.

For any
M,T_{0},T>0
, there exists a coupling of
\{\xi^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

and
\{\xi^{t}_{M,T_{0}}\}_{t\geq 0}
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}+T]}\mathbb{E}(\xi^{t}-\xi^{t}_{M,T_{0}})^{2}\leq Ce^{CT}(\varepsilon(M)+\sqrt{T}\,\varepsilon(T_{0}))

where
\varepsilon(M)
does not depend on
T_{0},T
and

\varepsilon(T_{0})
does not depend on
M,T
, and

\lim_{M\to\infty}\varepsilon(M)=0
and
\lim_{T_{0}\to\infty}\varepsilon(T_{0})=0
.

Proof.

Applying the approximation (
30
)
and arguments analogous to Lemma
3.2
, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{s,t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}|\mathbb{E}[v_{M}^{t}v_{M}^{s}]-\mathbb{E}[v^{t}v^{s}]|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}|C_{\theta}^{(M)}(t,s)-C_{\theta}(t,s)|+|c_{\theta}(*)-C_{\theta}(t,*)|+|c_{\theta}(*)-C_{\theta}(s,*)|\leq\varepsilon(M)+\varepsilon(T_{0}),

and hence there exists a coupling of
\{v_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

and
\{v^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
such that

\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\mathbb{E}(v^{t}-v_{M}^{t})^{2}\leq\varepsilon(M)+\sqrt{T}\,\varepsilon(T_{0}).

We bound
\xi^{t}-\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}
under this coupling of
\{v^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

with
\{v_{M}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
:
Let us write
\tilde{\xi}^{t}=\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}
. We have

\xi^{t}=\tilde{\xi}^{t}
for
t\in[0,T_{0})
, while for
t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]
,

\mathbb{E}(\xi^{t}-\tilde{\xi}^{t})^{2}\leq 3\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}R_{\theta}^{(M)}(t-s)|\xi^{s}-\tilde{\xi}^{s}|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}+\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|R_{\theta}(t,s)-R_{\theta}^{(M)}(t-s)||\xi^{s}|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}+\mathbb{E}(v^{t}-v_{M}^{t})^{2}\Big].

(87)

From the explicit definition of
R^{(M)}_{\theta}(t-s)
, the first term of
(
87
) satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|R_{\theta}^{(M)}(t-s)||\xi^{s}-\tilde{\xi}^{s}|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{T_{0}}^{t}|R_{\theta}^{(M)}(t-s)||\xi^{s}-\tilde{\xi}^{s}|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}\leq C\int_{T_{0}}^{t}\mathbb{E}(\xi^{s}-\tilde{\xi}^{s})^{2}\mathrm{d}s

for a constant
C>0
. Following the argument used to bound
(
74
), the second term of (
87
) is bounded by

\varepsilon(M)+C\varepsilon(t)^{2}
where
\varepsilon(t)\to 0
as
t\to\infty
, while the third term is bounded by

\varepsilon(M)+\sqrt{T}\,\varepsilon(T_{0})
under the above coupling. Then
by Gronwall’s inequality,

\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\mathbb{E}(\xi^{t}-\tilde{\xi}^{t})^{2}\leq Ce^{CT}\Big(\varepsilon(M)+\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\varepsilon(t)^{2}+\sqrt{T}\,\varepsilon(T_{0})\Big),

which implies the lemma upon adjusting
\varepsilon(T_{0})
.
∎

3.2.2
Convergence of the auxiliary process

Lemma 3.9
.

The value
\sigma_{Z}^{2}=\mathbb{E}{\theta^{*}}^{2}+c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)-2c_{\theta}(\ast)+\sigma^{2}
is positive.

Proof.

Let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the Langevin diffusion
(
7
) for which
the DMFT system of Theorem
2.5
is the large-
(n,d)

limit. By Theorem
4.3
to follow,

C_{\theta}(t,s)-C_{\theta}(t,*)-C_{\theta}(s,*)+\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\theta_{i}^{t}-\theta_{i}^{*})(\theta_{i}^{s}-\theta_{i}^{*})\Bigg]

Since
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is Markovian (conditional on

\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y},\bm{\theta}^{*}
), we have for all
t\geq s
that

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\theta_{i}^{t}-\theta_{i}^{*})(\theta_{i}^{s}-\theta_{i}^{*})\Bigg]=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\theta_{i}^{t}-\theta_{i}^{*})(\theta_{i}^{s}-\theta_{i}^{*})\;\Bigg|\;\bm{\theta}^{s},\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y},\bm{\theta}^{*}\Bigg]\Bigg]=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\theta_{i}^{s}-\theta_{i}^{*})^{2}\Bigg]\geq 0,

hence
C_{\theta}(t,s)-C_{\theta}(t,*)-C_{\theta}(s,*)+\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}\geq 0
.
Setting
s=t/2
and taking the limit
t\to\infty
under Definition

2.4
shows
c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)-2c_{\theta}(*)+\mathbb{E}{\theta^{*}}^{2}\geq 0
, and the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
c=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{M})
,
A=\diag(a_{1},\ldots,a_{M})
,
\Lambda=A+cc^{\top}/\sigma^{2}
, and consider the 2-dimensional Gaussian law

\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{M})
with

\displaystyle\Sigma_{M}=\begin{pmatrix}\rho_{M}^{2}&\kappa_{M}\\
\kappa_{M}&\rho_{M}^{2}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\kappa_{M}=\sigma_{Z}^{2}\cdot\Big[1-c^{\top}\Lambda^{-1}c/\sigma^{2}\Big]^{2},\qquad\rho^{2}_{M}=\kappa_{M}+c^{\top}\Lambda^{-1}c,

where
\sigma_{Z}^{2}=\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}+c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)-2c_{\theta}(\ast)+\sigma^{2}
.
Then there exists an error
\varepsilon(T)
not depending on
T_{0},M
and satisfying

\lim_{T\to\infty}\varepsilon(T)=0
, such that for any
M,T_{0},T,T^{\prime}>0
,

W_{2}(\mathsf{P}(\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{T_{0}+T},\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}),\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{M}))\leq\varepsilon(T)+\varepsilon(T^{\prime}).

Proof.

Let
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{Z}^{2})
, where
\sigma_{Z}^{2}>0
by Lemma

3.9
, and let
\{b_{m}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
for
m=1,\ldots,M
be
standard Brownian motions. We assume these are independent of each other and of

\{\xi^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
. Then the law of

\{\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
coincides with
the marginal law of
\{\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
in the joint process

\displaystyle\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}

\displaystyle=\xi^{t}\text{ for }t\in[0,T_{0})

\displaystyle\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}x_{m}^{t}+z\text{ for }t\geq T_{0}

(88)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}x_{m}^{t}

\displaystyle=-[a_{m}x_{m}^{t}+c_{m}\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}/\sigma^{2}]\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b_{m}^{t}\text{ for }1\leq m\leq M,\,t\geq 0

(89)

with initial conditions
x_{1}^{0}=\ldots=x_{M}^{0}=0
. Indeed,
given
\{\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
, the equations (
89
)
for
\{x_{m}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
have the explicit solutions

\displaystyle x_{m}^{t}=-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}c_{m}e^{-a_{m}(t-s)}\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{s}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-a_{m}(t-s)}\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b_{m}^{s},

and substituting this into (
88
) gives (
86
) upon
identifying
v_{M}^{t}=z+\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}e^{-a_{m}(t-s)}\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b_{m}^{s}
. It is direct to check that
\{v_{M}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
thus defined
has covariance
C_{\theta}^{(M)}(t,s)-2c_{\theta}(*)+\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}+\sigma^{2}
,
so this coincides with the law of
\{\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

defined by (
86
).

Let us denote
\tilde{\xi}^{t}=\xi_{M,T_{0}}^{t}
,

x^{t}=(x_{1}^{t},\ldots,x_{M}^{t})
, and
b^{t}=(b_{1}^{t},\ldots,b_{M}^{t})
. For
t\geq T_{0}
,
the evolution of
(\tilde{\xi}^{t},x^{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{M+1}
is a (Markovian)
Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process. Substituting (
88
) into
(
89
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}x^{t}=-[\Lambda x^{t}+cz/\sigma^{2}]\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b^{t}\text{ for }t\geq T_{0}

where
c=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{M})
and
\Lambda=A+cc^{\top}/\sigma^{2}
with

A=\diag(a_{1},\ldots,a_{M})
. This has the solution, for
t\geq T_{0}
,

\displaystyle x^{t}=e^{-\Lambda(t-T_{0})}x^{T_{0}}+\frac{z}{\sigma^{2}}\Lambda^{-1}(e^{-\Lambda(t-T_{0})}-I)c+\int_{T_{0}}^{t}e^{-\Lambda(t-s)}\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b^{s}.

Substituting back into (
88
),

\tilde{\xi}^{t}=c^{\top}e^{-\Lambda(t-T_{0})}x^{T_{0}}+z\Big[1+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}c^{\top}\Lambda^{-1}(e^{-\Lambda(t-T_{0})}-I)c\Big]+\int_{T_{0}}^{t}c^{\top}e^{-\Lambda(t-s)}\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b^{s}\text{ for }t\geq T_{0}.

(90)

Here, we note that the equation (
85
) implies that

\{\xi^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is itself a Gaussian process
(given by a linear functional of
\{v^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
),
so
x^{T_{0}}
with coordinates

x_{m}^{T_{0}}=\underbrace{-\frac{c_{m}}{\sigma^{2}}\int_{0}^{T_{0}}e^{-a_{m}(T_{0}-s)}\xi^{s}\mathrm{d}s}_{=U_{m}}+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{T_{0}}e^{-a_{m}(T_{0}-s)}\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}b^{s}_{m}}_{=V_{m}}

(91)

is a Gaussian vector. Consequently, the form (
90
) shows
that for any
T,T^{\prime}>0
,

(\tilde{\xi}^{T_{0}+T},\tilde{\xi}^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}})
has a centered
bivariate Gaussian law. To conclude the proof of the lemma, it suffices to show

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[(\tilde{\xi}^{T_{0}+T})^{2}]-\rho_{M}^{2}|,\,|\mathbb{E}[(\tilde{\xi}^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}})^{2}]-\rho_{M}^{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon(T)+\varepsilon(T^{\prime})

(92)

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\xi}^{T_{0}+T}\tilde{\xi}^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}]-\kappa_{M}|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon(T)+\varepsilon(T^{\prime})

(93)

for some errors
\varepsilon(T),\varepsilon(T^{\prime})
that hold uniformly over all
M,T_{0}>0
.

For (
92
), we may compute from the solution
(
90
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\tilde{\xi}^{T_{0}+T})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{c^{\top}e^{-\Lambda T}\mathbb{E}[x^{T_{0}}(x^{T_{0}})^{\top}]e^{-\Lambda T}c}_{=\mathrm{I}}+\underbrace{\sigma_{Z}^{2}\cdot\Big[1+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}c^{\top}\Lambda^{-1}(e^{-\Lambda T}-I)c\Big]^{2}+c^{\top}\Lambda^{-1}(I-e^{-2\Lambda T})c}_{=\mathrm{II}}.

Observe that
\|\Lambda^{-1/2}c\|_{2}^{2}\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}/a_{m}=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mu_{\theta}([a_{m-1},a_{m}))\leq\mu_{\theta}([\iota,\infty))
.
Hence
\|\Lambda^{-1/2}c\|_{2}\leq C
for a constant
C>0
not depending on
M
.
Since also
\lambda_{\min}(\Lambda)\geq\iota>0
, we have
\|e^{-\Lambda T}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq e^{-\iota T}
, so

|\mathrm{II}-\rho_{M}^{2}|\leq\varepsilon(T)

for an error
\varepsilon(T)
not depending on
M
.
To bound
\mathrm{I}
, write
x^{T_{0}}=U+V
where

U,V\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
have the coordinates
U_{m},V_{m}
in (
91
).
Then, from the bound
\mathbb{E}[(u^{\top}x^{T_{0}})^{2}]\leq 2\mathbb{E}[(u^{\top}U)^{2}]+2\mathbb{E}[(u^{\top}V)^{2}]
for each unit vector
u\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
, we have

\|\mathbb{E}[x^{T_{0}}(x^{T_{0}})^{\top}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\|\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}+2\|\mathbb{E}[VV^{\top}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

For the second term,

\|\mathbb{E}[VV^{\top}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|\diag(a_{m}^{-1}(1-e^{-a_{m}T_{0}}))\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\iota^{-1}
. For the first term,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\mathbb{E}\|U\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{c_{m}^{2}}{\sigma^{4}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T_{0}}e^{-a_{m}(T_{0}-s)}\xi^{s}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}\frac{c_{m}^{2}}{\sigma^{4}}\int_{0}^{T_{0}}e^{-a_{m}(T_{0}-s)}\mathrm{d}s\cdot\int_{0}^{T_{0}}e^{-a_{m}(T_{0}-s)}\mathbb{E}(\xi^{s})^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

Noting that

\mathbb{E}(\xi^{t})^{2}=(\sigma^{4}/\delta)C_{\eta}(t,t)\leq C

for all
t\geq 0
under Definition
2.4
,
this gives
\|\mathbb{E}[UU^{\top}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C^{\prime}\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}/a_{m}^{2}\leq C^{\prime}\mu_{\theta}([\iota,\infty))/\iota
. Combining these bounds shows

\|\mathbb{E}[x^{T_{0}}(x^{T_{0}})^{\top}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C
for a constant
C>0
not depending
on
M,T_{0}
. Then, combining with the previous bounds
\|\Lambda^{-1/2}u\|_{2}\leq C
and
\lambda_{\min}(\Lambda)\geq\iota
,
this shows
|\mathrm{I}|\leq\varepsilon(T)
, so

|\mathbb{E}[(\tilde{\xi}^{T_{0}+T})^{2}]-\rho_{M}^{2}|\leq\varepsilon(T)
.
The bound for
\mathbb{E}[(\tilde{\xi}^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}})^{2}]
in
(
92
) holds similarly.

For (
93
), we may compute similarly from
(
90
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\tilde{\xi}^{T_{0}+T})\tilde{\xi}^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle=u^{\top}e^{-\Lambda T}\mathbb{E}[x^{T_{0}}(x^{T_{0}})^{\top}]e^{-\Lambda(T+T^{\prime})}u

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma_{Z}^{2}\cdot\Big[1+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}u^{\top}\Lambda^{-1}(e^{-\Lambda(T+T^{\prime})}-I)u\Big]\Big[1+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}u^{\top}\Lambda^{-1}(e^{-\Lambda T}-I)u\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+u^{\top}\Lambda^{-1}(e^{-\Lambda T^{\prime}}-e^{-\Lambda(2T+T^{\prime})})u,

and the arguments to show (
93
) from this form
are the same as above.
∎

Lemma 3.11
.

Consider the 2-dimensional Gaussian law
\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{\infty})
with

\Sigma_{\infty}=\begin{pmatrix}\rho^{2}_{\infty}&\kappa_{\infty}\\
\kappa_{\infty}&\rho^{2}_{\infty}\end{pmatrix},\quad\kappa_{\infty}=\frac{\mathbb{E}{\theta^{*}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}+c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)-2c_{\theta}(\ast)}{(1+\sigma^{-2}(c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))^{2}},\quad\rho^{2}_{\infty}=\kappa_{\infty}+\frac{c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)}{1+\sigma^{-2}(c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))}.

Then
\lim_{M\to\infty}\|\Sigma_{M}-\Sigma_{\infty}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=0
.

Proof.

This follows from noting that
c^{\top}\Lambda^{-1}c=\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}/a_{m}}{1+\sigma^{-2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}/a_{m}}
via the Sherman-Morrison
identity, and

\sum_{m=1}^{M}c_{m}^{2}/a_{m}\rightarrow\int_{\iota}^{\infty}\mu_{\theta}(\mathrm{d}a)=c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
as
M\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

We now complete the proof of Lemma
3.7
.

Proof of Lemma
3.7
.

By Lemmas
3.8
,
3.10
, and

3.11
, it holds that

W_{2}(\mathsf{P}(\xi^{T_{0}+T},\xi^{T_{0}+T+T^{\prime}}),\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{\infty}))\leq Ce^{C(T+T^{\prime})}(\varepsilon(M)+\sqrt{T+T^{\prime}}\,\varepsilon(T_{0}))+\varepsilon(T)+\varepsilon(T^{\prime})+\varepsilon(M).

Taking first the limit
M\to\infty
, then
choosing
T=T^{\prime}=t
and
T_{0}\equiv T_{0}(t)
such that
\lim_{t\to\infty}T_{0}(t)=\infty
and
\lim_{t\to\infty}e^{2Ct}\sqrt{2t}\,\varepsilon(T_{0}(t))=0

and taking
t\to\infty
, this shows

W_{2}(\mathsf{P}(\xi^{T_{0}(t)+t},\xi^{T_{0}(t)+2t}),\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{\infty}))\rightarrow 0
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. Under Definition
2.4
,
this implies

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\delta}c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\delta}C_{\eta}(T_{0}(t)+t,T_{0}(t)+t)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[(\xi^{T_{0}(t)+t})^{2}]=\rho^{2}_{\infty},

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\delta}c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\delta}C_{\eta}(T_{0}(t)+t,T_{0}(t)+2t)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[\xi^{T_{0}(t)+t}\xi^{T_{0}(t)+2t}]=\kappa_{\infty}.

This shows the desired forms of
c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)
and
c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
,
and we have also from these forms that

c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)=\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}\bigg[\frac{\sigma^{-2}(c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))}{1+\sigma^{-2}(c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))}\bigg]<\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}.

∎

3.3
Completing the proof

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.

By Lemmas
3.1
and
3.7
, we have five
equations (
60
), (
83
),
(
84
) for the five variables
c^{\mathrm{tti}}_{\theta}(0),c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty),c_{\theta}(\ast),c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0),c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
.
Defining
\mse,\mse_{*}
by (
42
), these equations show

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}-(c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))=\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}+(c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))}=\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}+\mse},

\displaystyle\omega_{\ast}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega^{2}}{c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)}=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}{\theta^{*}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}+c_{\theta}(\infty)-2c_{\theta}(\ast)}=\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}+\mse_{\ast}},

as well as

\displaystyle\mse

\displaystyle=c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)=\mathbb{E}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast}}[\langle\theta^{2}\rangle_{g,\omega}-\langle\theta\rangle_{g,\omega}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast}}\langle(\theta-\langle\theta\rangle_{g,\omega})^{2}\rangle_{g,\omega},

\displaystyle\mse_{\ast}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}{\theta^{\ast}}^{2}-2\mathbb{E}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast}}[\theta^{\ast}\langle\theta\rangle_{g,\omega}]+\mathbb{E}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast}}\langle\theta\rangle_{g,\omega}^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{g_{\ast},\omega_{\ast}}(\theta^{\ast}-\langle\theta\rangle_{g,\omega})^{2}.

This verifies that the fixed-point equations (
43
) hold,
where it is clear that
\omega,\omega_{*}
are uniquely defined from
\mse,\mse_{*}

via (
43
).
Defining
\ymse,\ymse_{*}
by (
42
), we have also from the above forms
of
\omega,\omega_{*}
that

\displaystyle\ymse

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\delta}(c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))=\sigma^{2}\Big(1-\frac{\omega\sigma^{2}}{\delta}\Big),

\displaystyle\ymse^{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\delta}(2c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))-\sigma^{2}=\sigma^{2}+\frac{\omega\sigma^{4}}{\delta}\Big(\frac{\omega}{\omega_{*}}-2\Big),

verifying (
44
). Finally, the statement
(
45
)
is a consequence of (
82
) shown in the proof of Lemma

3.1
.
∎

4
Analysis of fixed-prior Langevin dynamics under
LSI

In this section, we prove Theorem
2.9

and Corollary
2.10
that verify Definition

2.4
and deduce the replica-symmetric limits for the
Bayes-optimal mean-squared-errors and free energy, under Assumption

2.7
of a log-Sobolev inequality (LSI) for the posterior law.

4.1
Preliminaries

4.1.1
Properties of Langevin dynamics

We review in this section two general results on a Langevin diffusion
of the form

\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}^{t}=\nabla U(\bm{\theta}^{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{t}

(94)

with an equilibrium measure
e^{U(\bm{\theta})}
. The first is
a fluctuation-dissipation relation for its
correlation and response functions at equilibrium, and the second is
a Bismut-Elworthy-Li representation for the spatial derivative
of its Markov semigroup. For bounded observables, similar
fluctuation-dissipation theorems have been stated and shown
in
[
80
,
81
]
and Bismut-Elworthy-Li
formulae in
[
82
,
83
]
.
We give versions of these results here for a class of unbounded observables
which may have linear growth

\mathcal{A}=\{f\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}):\nabla f,\nabla^{2}f\text{ are globally bounded}\},

and a class of drift coefficients

\mathcal{B}=\{U\in C^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}):\nabla^{2}U,\nabla^{3}U\text{ are globally
bounded and H\"{o}lder continuous}\},

(95)

drawing upon some analyses of our companion work
[
27
, Appendix A]
.

We write

P_{t}f(\bm{\theta})=\mathbb{E}[f(\bm{\theta}^{t})\mid\bm{\theta}^{0}=\bm{\theta}],\qquad\mathrm{L}f(\bm{\theta})=\nabla U^{\top}\nabla f(\bm{\theta})+\Tr\nabla^{2}f(\bm{\theta})

for the Markov semigroup and infinitesimal generator
associated to (
94
).
It is shown in
[
27
, Proposition A.2]
that

f\in\mathcal{A},\,U\in\mathcal{B}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\nabla P_{t}f(\bm{\theta}),\nabla^{2}P_{t}f(\bm{\theta})\text{ are uniformly bounded over }t\in[0,T],\,\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

(96)

for any fixed
T>0
. In particular,
P_{t}f\in\mathcal{A}
for each fixed
t>0
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Suppose
U\in\mathcal{B}
, and (
94
) has the unique
stationary distribution
q(\bm{\theta})=e^{U(\bm{\theta})}
with finite third moments.
Let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the solution to (
94
)
with initial condition
\bm{\theta}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
, and let
A\in\mathcal{A}
and
B\in\mathcal{B}
.

(a)

Define the response function

R_{AB}^{\mathbf{x}}(t,s)=P_{s}(\nabla B^{\top}\nabla P_{t-s}A)(\mathbf{x})
.
Then
R_{AB}^{\mathbf{x}}(t,s)
satisfies the following condition:
Fix any continuous bounded function
h:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
. For each
\varepsilon>0
,
let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}\}_{t\geq 0}
denote
the solution of the perturbed dynamics

\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}=\nabla[U(\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon})+\varepsilon h(t)B(\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon})]\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{t}

with the same initial condition
\bm{\theta}^{0,\varepsilon}=\mathbf{x}
.
Then for any
t>0
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\mathbb{E}[A(\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon})\mid\bm{\theta}^{0,\varepsilon}=\mathbf{x}]-\mathbb{E}[A(\bm{\theta}^{t})\mid\bm{\theta}^{0}=\mathbf{x}]\Big)=\int_{0}^{t}R_{AB}^{\mathbf{x}}(t,s)h(s)\mathrm{d}s.

(b)

Define the correlation function

C_{AB}^{\mathbf{x}}(t,s)=\mathbb{E}[A(\bm{\theta}^{t})B(\bm{\theta}^{s})\mid\bm{\theta}^{0}=\mathbf{x}]
.
Then for any
t\geq s\geq 0
, averaging over an initial condition

\mathbf{x}\sim q
drawn from the stationary distribution,

\partial_{t}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim q}C_{AB}^{\mathbf{x}}(t,s)={-}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim q}R_{AB}^{\mathbf{x}}(t,s).

Proof.

Part (a) is an application of
[
27
, Proposition A.1]
of our companion
paper (specialized to this setting of dynamics with a fixed and non-adaptive
prior).

For part (b), we will use also from
[
27
, Proposition A.2]
that
for
A\in\mathcal{A}
, we have
\partial_{t}P_{t}A=\mathrm{L}P_{t}A
.
Since the dynamics (
103
)
are Markovian with stationary distribution
q(\bm{\theta})
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim q}C_{AB}^{\mathbf{x}}(t,s)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim q}[\mathbb{E}[A(\bm{\theta}^{t-s})\mid\bm{\theta}^{0}=\mathbf{x}]B(\mathbf{x})]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim q}(B\cdot P_{t-s}A)[\mathbf{x}].

To differentiate under the integral in
t
, note that

\partial_{t}(B\cdot P_{t-s}A)=B\cdot\mathrm{L}P_{t-s}A
.
By the uniform boundedness of

\nabla P_{t}A,\nabla^{2}P_{t}A
over
t\in[0,T]
, the Lipschitz-continuity of

\nabla B,\nabla U
, and finiteness of third moments of
q
,
we have that
(B\cdot\mathrm{L}P_{t}A)[\mathbf{x}]
is uniformly integrable
with respect to
\mathbf{x}\sim q
over
t\in[0,T]
.
Thus dominated convergence applies to show

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim q}C_{AB}^{\mathbf{x}}(t,s)=\partial_{t}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim q}(B\cdot P_{t-s}A)[\mathbf{x}]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim q}(B\cdot\mathrm{L}P_{t-s}A)[\mathbf{x}].

On the other hand, using also that both

\nabla B^{\top}\nabla P_{t}A
and
B\cdot\mathrm{L}P_{t}A
are
integrable with respect to
\mathbf{x}\sim q
, we have via integration-by-parts

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim q}R_{AB}^{\mathbf{x}}(t,s)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim q}(\nabla B^{\top}\nabla P_{t-s}A)[\mathbf{x}]=\int q(\bm{\theta})(\nabla B^{\top}\nabla P_{t-s}A)[\bm{\theta}]\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle=-\int B(\bm{\theta})\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}[q\,\partial_{j}(P_{t-s}A)](\bm{\theta})\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle=-\int B(\bm{\theta})\,\big[q\,\Tr\nabla^{2}(P_{t-s}A)+\nabla(P_{t-s}A)^{\top}\nabla q\big](\bm{\theta})\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle=-\int q(\bm{\theta})B(\bm{\theta})\,\big[\Tr\nabla^{2}(P_{t-s}A)+\nabla(P_{t-s}A)^{\top}\nabla\log q\big](\bm{\theta})\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim q}(B\cdot\mathrm{L}P_{t-s}A)[\mathbf{x}].

∎

Lemma 4.2
.

Suppose
U\in\mathcal{B}
, and consider the solution

(\bm{\theta}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
to

\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}^{t}=\nabla U(\bm{\theta}^{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{t},\qquad\mathrm{d}\mathbf{V}^{t}=[\nabla^{2}U(\bm{\theta}^{t})]\mathbf{V}^{t}\mathrm{d}t

(97)

with initial condition
(\bm{\theta}^{0},\mathbf{V}^{0})=(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{I})
,
adapted to the canonical filtration of the Brownian motion

\{\mathbf{b}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
. Then for any
f\in\mathcal{A}
and any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\nabla P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{V}^{t\top}\,\nabla f(\bm{\theta}^{t})\mid(\bm{\theta}^{0},\mathbf{V}^{0})=(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{I})]

(98)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t\sqrt{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[f(\bm{\theta}^{t})\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbf{V}^{s}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}\;\bigg|\;(\bm{\theta}^{0},\mathbf{V}^{0})=(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{I})\bigg]

(99)

Proof.

The first identity (
98
) is the statement of
[
27
, Eq. (184)]

(again specialized to this setting of dynamics with a fixed and non-adaptive
prior).

For the second identity (
99
), we use from
[
27
, Proposition
A.2]
that for
f\in\mathcal{A}
and any fixed
t\geq 0
,

(s,\bm{\theta})\mapsto P_{t-s}f(\bm{\theta})
is
C^{1}
in
s\in[0,t]
and

C^{2}
in
\bm{\theta}
, with
\partial_{s}P_{t-s}f(\bm{\theta})={-}\mathrm{L}P_{t-s}f(\bm{\theta})
. Then Itô’s formula applied to
g(s,\bm{\theta})=P_{t-s}f(\bm{\theta})
gives

\displaystyle f(\bm{\theta}^{t})=g(t,\bm{\theta}^{t})

\displaystyle=g(0,\bm{\theta}^{0})+\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{s}g(s,\bm{\theta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}g(s,\bm{\theta}^{s})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}^{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\Tr\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}^{2}g(s,\bm{\theta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})+\int_{0}^{t}(\partial_{s}+\mathrm{L})P_{t-s}f(\bm{\theta}^{s})\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{t}\nabla P_{t-s}f(\bm{\theta}^{s})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}

\displaystyle=P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})+\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{t}\nabla P_{t-s}f(\bm{\theta}^{s})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}.

Since
\nabla^{2}U
is bounded,
\{\mathbf{V}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
is bounded over finite
time horizons, so
\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{V}^{s\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}
is a martingale.
Multiplying both sides by this martingale and taking expectations gives

\mathbb{E}\bigg[f(\bm{\theta}^{t})\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbf{V}^{s}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}\;\bigg|\;(\bm{\theta}^{0},\mathbf{V}^{0})=(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{I})\bigg]=\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{V}^{s}}^{\top}\nabla P_{t-s}f(\bm{\theta}^{s})\mid(\bm{\theta}^{0},\mathbf{V}^{0})=(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{I})]\mathrm{d}s.

Since
P_{t}f\in\mathcal{A}
, we may apply (
98
) with
P_{t-s}f
in place
of
f
to get

\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{V}^{s}}^{\top}\nabla P_{t-s}f(\bm{\theta}^{s})\mid(\bm{\theta}^{0},\mathbf{V}^{0})=(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{I})]\mathrm{d}s=\int_{0}^{t}\nabla P_{s}(P_{t-s}f)(\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}s=t\cdot\nabla P_{t}f(\mathbf{x}).

Substituting above and rearranging shows (
99
).
∎

4.1.2
Interpretation of the DMFT correlation and
response

We remark that under Assumption
2.2
(a), the log-posterior density

\log\mathsf{P}_{g}(\bm{\theta}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})
belongs to the function class
\mathcal{B}
, and

\mathsf{P}_{g}(\bm{\theta}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})
is the unique stationary distribution of
(
7
).
Fixing
\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y},\bm{\theta}^{*}
, consider the coordinate functions

e_{j}(\bm{\theta})=\theta_{j},\qquad e_{j}^{*}(\bm{\theta})=\theta_{j}^{*},\qquad x_{i}(\bm{\theta})=\frac{\sqrt{\delta}}{\sigma^{2}}([\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}]_{i}-y_{i}).

(100)

(Here,
e_{j}^{*}
is a constant function not depending on
\bm{\theta}
.)
We define their associated correlation and response matrices

\begin{gathered}\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,s)=(C_{e_{j}e_{k}}^{\bm{\theta}_{0}}(t,s))_{j,k=1}^{d},\quad\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,*)=(C_{e_{j}e_{k}^{*}}^{\bm{\theta}_{0}}(t,0))_{j,k=1}^{d},\quad\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)=(R_{e_{j}e_{k}}^{\bm{\theta}_{0}}(t,s))_{j,k=1}^{d}\\
\mathbf{C}_{\eta}(t,s)=(C_{x_{j}x_{k}}^{\bm{\theta}_{0}}(t,s))_{j,k=1}^{n},\qquad\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(t,s)=(R_{x_{j}x_{k}}^{\bm{\theta}_{0}}(t,s))_{j,k=1}^{n}\end{gathered}

(101)

where
C_{AB}^{\bm{\theta}_{0}}(t,s)
and
R_{AB}^{\bm{\theta}_{0}}(t,s)

are the correlation and response functions as defined
in Lemma
4.1
for these coordinate functions,
under the dynamics (
7
) with fixed prior
g(\cdot)

and the given initial condition
\bm{\theta}^{0}
of Assumption
2.1
.

The following result is a direct application of
[
27
, Theorem 2.8]
.

Theorem 4.3

(
[
27
]
)
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.1
and
2.2
(a) hold, and let

C_{\theta},C_{\eta},R_{\theta},R_{\eta}
be the correlation and response functions of
the DMFT system in Theorem
2.3
(a) approximating the dynamics
(
7
). Then almost surely as
n,d\to\infty
,

\begin{gathered}d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,s)\to C_{\theta}(t,s),\qquad d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,*)\to C_{\theta}(t,*),\qquad n^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\eta}(t,s)\to C_{\eta}(t,s)\\
d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)\to R_{\theta}(t,s),\qquad n^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(t,s)\to R_{\eta}(t,s).\end{gathered}

4.2
Posterior bounds and Wasserstein-2
convergence

Fixing the prior
g(\cdot)
and the data
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})
, let us write for convenience

q(\bm{\theta})=\mathsf{P}_{g}(\bm{\theta}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})\propto\exp\bigg({-}\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\log g(\theta_{j})\bigg)

(102)

for the posterior density. The Langevin diffusion (
7
)
with this fixed prior is then

\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}^{t}=\nabla\log q(\bm{\theta}^{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{t}.

(103)

We will use the notations

\displaystyle\langle f(\bm{\theta})\rangle=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q}[f(\bm{\theta})],\qquad P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\langle f(\bm{\theta}^{t})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}=\mathbb{E}[f(\bm{\theta}^{t})\mid\bm{\theta}^{0}=\mathbf{x}]

where the former is an average under the posterior law
q(\cdot)
conditional on

\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y}
, and the latter is an average over
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

solving (
103
) conditional on
\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y}
and also
the initial condition
\bm{\theta}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
. We write as shorthand

P_{t}(\mathbf{x})=\langle\bm{\theta}^{t}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}=(P_{t}e_{1},\ldots,P_{t}e_{d})[\mathbf{x}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

We reserve
\langle f(\bm{\theta}^{t})\rangle
for the expectation conditional on

\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y}
but averaging also over
\bm{\theta}^{0}
.

For constants
C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}}>0
, define the
(\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
-dependent event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})

\displaystyle=\Big\{\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0},\;\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2},\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{0}d,\;\text{the LSI }(\ref{eq:LSI})\text{ holds for }q(\bm{\theta})\Big\}.

(104)

Note that under Assumptions
2.1
and
2.7
(a),
this event holds almost surely for all large
n,d
for some
sufficiently large choices of constants
C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}}>0
.
All subsequent constants
C,C^{\prime},c,c^{\prime}>0
in this section may change from
instance to instance, and are dimension-free and depend only on

C_{0},C_{\text{LSI}}\text{ above},\;\delta,\sigma^{2},g_{*}\text{ of Assumption \ref{assump:model}},\;C,c_{0},r_{0}\text{ of Assumption \ref{assump:prior}(a)},\text{ and }\log g(0).

(105)
