We record the following elementary bounds for the posterior expectation

\langle f(\bm{\theta})\rangle=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q}f(\bm{\theta})
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Suppose Assumption
2.2
(a) holds. Then on
the event where
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
, there exists a constant
C>0
for which

\displaystyle\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C(d+\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}),

(106)

\displaystyle\langle\|\nabla\log q(\bm{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C(d+\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}),

(107)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\log q(\bm{\theta})\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq C.

(108)

In particular, on
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
, for a constant
C^{\prime}>0
we have

\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq C^{\prime}d
and

\langle\|\nabla\log q(\bm{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq C^{\prime}d
.

Proof.

(
108
) is immediate from the form of
\log q(\bm{\theta})
, the bound
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
,
and Assumption
2.2
(a).

For (
106
), write
\mathbb{E}_{g},\mathbb{P}_{g}
for the expectation
and probability over the prior
\theta\sim g
and

\theta_{j}\overset{iid}{\sim}g
. We note that under
Assumption
2.2
(a), we have

\log g(\theta)=\log g(0)+\theta(\log g)^{\prime}(0)+\int_{0}^{\theta}\int_{0}^{x}(\log g)^{\prime\prime}(u)\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C(1+|\theta|)-(c_{0}/2)(|\theta|-r_{0})^{2}\leq C^{\prime}-c^{\prime}\theta^{2}

for some constants
C,C^{\prime},c^{\prime}>0
depending only on the constants of
Assumption
2.2
(a) and on
\log g(0)
. Then

g
is subgaussian, and for some constants
C,c>0

(c.f.
[
84
, Eq. (3.1)]
)

\mathbb{E}_{g}\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\leq Cd,\qquad\mathbb{P}_{g}[\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{g}\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\geq du]\leq Ce^{-cdu}\text{ for all }u\geq 1.

(109)

Write

q(\bm{\theta})=\frac{1}{Z}\exp\bigg({-}\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\bigg)\prod_{j=1}^{d}g(\theta_{j}),\qquad Z=\mathbb{E}_{g}\bigg[\exp\bigg({-}\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\bigg)\bigg].

We have by Jensen’s inequality

{-}\log Z\leq\mathbb{E}_{g}[\|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}/2\sigma^{2}]\leq C(d+\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{g}\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2})\leq C^{\prime}(d+\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2})
. Then for any
M>0
, also bounding the
exponential from above by 1,

\Big\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}\{\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\geq M\}\Big\rangle\leq\frac{1}{Z}\,\mathbb{E}_{g}\Big[\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}\{\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\geq M\}\Big]\leq e^{C^{\prime}(d+\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2})}\mathbb{E}_{g}\Big[\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}\{\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\geq M\}\Big].

Integrating the tail bound (
109
) shows that this is less than

d+\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}

for
M=C(d+\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2})
and a sufficiently large choice of constant

C>0
. Thus

\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq M+\Big\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}\{\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\geq M\}\Big\rangle\leq C^{\prime}(d+\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}).

This shows (
106
). Since

\nabla\log q(\bm{\theta})
is
C
-Lipschitz by (
108
), and

\|\nabla\log q(0)\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}/\sigma^{2}\|_{2}^{2}+2d\cdot(\log g)^{\prime}(0)^{2}\leq C(d+\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2})
, the statement
(
107
) follows from (
106
).
∎

Remark 4.5
.

In a later proof, we will require that (
106
) holds in a
form

\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq Cd+(C/\sigma^{2})\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}

(110)

for all large noise variances
\sigma^{2}>0
, where
C>0
is a constant not
depending on
\sigma^{2}
. This may be seen from the above arguments:
Writing now
C,C^{\prime}>0
for constants not depending on
\sigma^{2}
,
the above shows
{-}\log Z\leq(C^{\prime}/\sigma^{2})(d+\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2})
,
and hence
\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}\{\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\geq M\}\rangle\leq d+\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}/\sigma^{2}
for
M=Cd+(C/\sigma^{2})\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}
with a sufficiently
large choice of constant
C>0
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Suppose Assumption
2.2
(a) holds.
Let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the solution to (
9
) with
initial condition
\bm{\theta}^{0}\sim q_{0}
, let
q_{t}(\bm{\theta}^{t})

be the law of
\bm{\theta}^{t}
, and let
W_{2}(\cdot)
the Wasserstein-2 distance,
all conditional on
\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}
(and averaging over
\bm{\theta}^{0}
).
Then on the event
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

W_{2}(q_{t},q)\leq Ce^{-(2/C_{\mathrm{LSI}})t}\,W_{2}(q_{0},q)\text{ for all }t\geq 0.

(111)

Proof.

For
t\in[0,1]
we may apply a simple synchronous coupling and
Grönwall argument: Let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and

\{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the solutions of (
9
)
with initial conditions
\bm{\theta}^{0}\sim q_{0}
and
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{0}\sim q
,
coupled by the same Brownian motion. Then

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|(\bm{\theta}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t})\|_{2}\leq\|\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(\bm{\theta}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t})\|_{2}=\|\nabla\log q(\bm{\theta}^{t})-\nabla\log q(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t})\|_{2}\leq C\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}\|_{2}
by definition
of the Langevin equation (
9
) and by (
108
).
Hence

\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}\|_{2}\leq e^{Ct}\|\bm{\theta}^{0}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{0}\|_{2}.

(112)

Letting
(\bm{\theta}^{0},\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{0})
be the coupling of
(q_{0},q)
for which

\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{0}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle=W_{2}(q_{0},q)^{2}
, we have that

(\bm{\theta}^{t},\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t})
is a coupling of
(q_{t},q)
, so

W_{2}(q_{t},q)^{2}\leq\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq e^{2Ct}\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{0}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle=e^{2Ct}W_{2}(q_{0},q)^{2}.

Thus
W_{2}(q_{t},q)\leq C^{\prime}W_{2}(q_{0},q)
for all
t\in[0,1]
, which implies
(
111
) for
t\in[0,1]
and some
C>0
.

For
t\geq 1
, under the curvature-dimension lower bound

-\nabla^{2}\log q(\bm{\theta})\succeq{-}L\operatorname{Id}
for a constant

L>0
that is implied by (
108
), we apply from
[
85
, Lemma
4.2]
that

\DKL(q_{1}\|q)\leq\left(\frac{1}{4\alpha}+\frac{L}{2}\right)W_{2}(q_{0},q)^{2},\qquad\alpha=\frac{e^{2L}-1}{2L}.

(113)

Under the LSI condition of
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
,
we have the exponential contraction of
relative entropy (c.f.
[
86
, Theorem 5.2.1]
)

\DKL(q_{t}\|q)\leq e^{-2(t-1)/C_{\mathrm{LSI}}}\DKL(q_{1}\|q)\text{ for all }t\geq 1.

(114)

We have also the
T_{2}
-transportation inequality
(c.f.
[
86
, Theorem 9.6.1]
)

W_{2}(q_{t},q)^{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{LSI}}\,\DKL(q_{t}\|q),

(115)

and (
111
) for
t\geq 1
follows
follows from combining (
113
), (
114
), and
(
115
).
∎

4.3
Properties of the correlation and response

In this section, on the event
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
,
we now show approximate time-translation-invariance at large
times for the correlation and response matrices

\mathbf{C}_{\theta},\mathbf{C}_{\eta},\mathbf{R}_{\theta},\mathbf{R}_{\eta}
defined in Section

4.1.2
. We may write these using our Markov semigroup
notation as

\begin{gathered}\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,s)=\Big(\big\langle e_{k}(\bm{\theta}^{s})P_{t-s}e_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{s})\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\Big)_{j,k=1}^{d},\qquad\mathbf{C}_{\eta}(t,s)=\Big(\big\langle x_{k}(\bm{\theta}^{s})P_{t-s}x_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{s})\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\Big)_{j,k=1}^{n},\\
\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)=\Big(\big\langle\nabla e_{k}(\bm{\theta}^{s})^{\top}\nabla P_{t-s}e_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{s})\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\Big)_{j,k=1}^{d},\qquad\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(t,s)=\Big(\langle\nabla x_{k}(\bm{\theta}^{s})^{\top}\nabla P_{t-s}x_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{s})\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\Big)_{j,k=1}^{n}.\end{gathered}

Lemma 4.7
.

Suppose Assumption
2.2
(a) holds.
Let
\mathbf{C}_{\theta},\mathbf{C}_{\eta},\mathbf{R}_{\theta},\mathbf{R}_{\eta}
be defined
for the dynamics (
7
), and set

\begin{gathered}\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)=\Big(\big\langle e_{k}(\bm{\theta})P_{\tau}e_{j}(\bm{\theta})\rangle\Big)_{j,k=1}^{d},\qquad\mathbf{C}_{\eta}^{\infty}(\tau)=\Big(\big\langle x_{k}(\bm{\theta})P_{\tau}x_{j}(\bm{\theta})\big\rangle\Big)_{j,k=1}^{n},\\
\qquad\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)=\Big(\big\langle\nabla e_{k}(\bm{\theta})^{\top}\nabla P_{\tau}e_{j}(\bm{\theta})\big\rangle\Big)_{j,k=1}^{d},\qquad\mathbf{R}_{\eta}^{\infty}(\tau)=\Big(\big\langle\nabla x_{k}(\bm{\theta})^{\top}\nabla P_{\tau}x_{j}(\bm{\theta})\big\rangle\Big)_{j,k=1}^{n}\end{gathered}

where
\langle\cdot\rangle
is expectation under the posterior law

q(\cdot)
.
Then on
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})\cap\{\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{0}d\}
,
there exist constants
C,c>0
such that for all
t\geq s\geq 0
,

\displaystyle|\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,s)-\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(t-s)|

\displaystyle\leq Cde^{-cs}

(116)

\displaystyle|\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)-\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\infty}(t-s)|

\displaystyle\leq Cde^{-cs}

(117)

\displaystyle|\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\eta}(t,s)-\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\eta}^{\infty}(t-s)|

\displaystyle\leq Cde^{-cs}

(118)

\displaystyle|\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(t,s)-\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}^{\infty}(t-s)|

\displaystyle\leq Cde^{-cs}

(119)

Proof.

Momentarily
let
q_{t}
be the law of
\bm{\theta}^{t}
conditional on
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})
and also on
a fixed initial condition
\bm{\theta}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
. For any fixed
t\geq 0
, denote by

\bm{\varphi}^{t}\sim q
a random vector such that

(\bm{\theta}^{t},\bm{\varphi}^{t})
is a coupling of
(q_{t},q)
for which

\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\varphi}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}=W_{2}(q_{t},q)^{2}
, where

W_{2}(\cdot)
is the Wasserstein-2 distance conditional on
\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y}
and

\bm{\theta}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
.
Then observe that for any
M
-Lipschitz function
f
, we have

\langle\|f(\bm{\theta}^{t})-f(\bm{\varphi}^{t})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\leq M^{2}\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\varphi}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}=M^{2}W_{2}(q_{t},q)^{2}.

(120)

Furthermore
W_{2}(q_{t},q)^{2}\leq Ce^{-ct}W_{2}(\delta_{\mathbf{x}},q)^{2}\leq 2Ce^{-ct}(\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}+\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle)

for all
t\geq 0
by Lemma
4.6
.
Then, applying (
120
)
with
f(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{x}
and
f(\mathbf{x})=\nabla\log q(\mathbf{x})
, and applying
(
106
–
108
) on the event

\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
, we have the basic estimates

\begin{gathered}\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\varphi}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\leq Ce^{-ct}(\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}+d),\quad\langle\|\nabla\log q(\bm{\theta}^{t})-\nabla\log q(\bm{\varphi}^{t})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\leq Ce^{-ct}(\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}+d),\\
\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\leq C(\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}+d),\quad\langle\|\nabla\log q(\bm{\theta}^{t})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\leq C(\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}+d)\\
\langle\|\bm{\varphi}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle=\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq Cd,\quad\langle\|\nabla\log q(\bm{\varphi}^{t})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle=\langle\|\nabla\log q(\bm{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq Cd.\end{gathered}

(121)

We note that also

\displaystyle\|P_{t}(\mathbf{x})-P_{t}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{Ct}\|\mathbf{x}-\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}^{2},

(122)

\displaystyle\|P_{t}(\mathbf{x})-P_{t}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-ct}(\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}^{2}+d).

(123)

Indeed, (
122
) follows from (
112
) and
Jensen’s inequality. Also by Jensen’s inequality and (
121
),

\displaystyle\|P_{t}(\mathbf{x})-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}=\|\langle\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\varphi}^{t}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq Ce^{-ct}(\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}+d),

(124)

and applying this bound for
both
P_{t}(\mathbf{x})
and
P_{t}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})
yields (
123
).

For (
116
), note that for any
s,\tau\geq 0
,

\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle e_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{s})P_{\tau}e_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{s})\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}.

(125)

Now let
q_{s}
be the law of
\bm{\theta}^{s}
conditional on
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})

and the given initial condition
\bm{\theta}^{0}
of Assumption
2.2
,
and let

(\bm{\theta}^{s},\bm{\varphi}^{s})
be the optimal Wasserstein-2 coupling of
(q_{s},q)

as above. Then

\displaystyle|\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)-\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\big\langle\big|e_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{s})P_{\tau}e_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-e_{j}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})P_{\tau}e_{j}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\big|\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\big\langle\big|(\theta_{j}^{s}-\varphi_{j}^{s})P_{\tau}e_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{s})\big|\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}+\big\langle\big|\varphi_{j}^{s}(P_{\tau}e_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-P_{\tau}e_{j}(\bm{\varphi}^{s}))\big|\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\big\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bm{\varphi}^{s}\|_{2}^{2}\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}^{1/2}\big\langle\|P_{\tau}(\bm{\theta}^{s})\|_{2}^{2}\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}^{1/2}}_{\mathrm{I}}+\underbrace{\big\langle\|\bm{\varphi}^{s}\|_{2}^{2}\big\rangle^{1/2}\big\langle\|P_{\tau}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-P_{\tau}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\|_{2}^{2}\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}^{1/2}}_{\mathrm{II}}

(126)

where we recall our shorthand

P_{t}(\mathbf{x})=\langle\bm{\theta}^{t}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

We have
\mathrm{I}\leq Cde^{-cs}
for all
s\geq 0
by
(
121
) and

\langle\|P_{\tau}(\bm{\theta}^{s})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\leq\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{s+\tau}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}

which follows from Jensen’s inequality. For
\mathrm{II}
,
by (
122
) and (
121
), we have

\big\langle\|P_{\tau}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-P_{\tau}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\|_{2}^{2}\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\leq e^{C\tau}\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bm{\varphi}^{s}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\leq e^{C\tau}\cdot Cde^{-cs}.

Choosing a large enough constant
s_{0}>0
, for
\tau\leq s/s_{0}
, this gives

\langle\|P_{\tau}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-P_{\tau}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\leq C^{\prime}de^{-c^{\prime}s}
. For
\tau>s/s_{0}
, applying instead
(
123
) and (
121
), we have

\langle\|P_{\tau}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-P_{\tau}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\leq Ce^{-c\tau}(\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{s}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}+\langle\|\bm{\varphi}^{s}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle+d)\leq C^{\prime}de^{-c^{\prime}s}
. Thus, for some
C,c>0
,

\big\langle\|P_{\tau}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-P_{\tau}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\|_{2}^{2}\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\leq Cde^{-cs}\text{ for all }s,\tau\geq 0.

Thus also
\mathrm{II}\leq Cde^{-cs}
, and applying these bounds for

\mathrm{I}
and
\mathrm{II}
to (
126
)
shows (
116
).

For (
117
), note that

\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle(\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j})[\bm{\theta}^{s}]\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}},\qquad\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle(\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j})[\bm{\theta}]\rangle.

(127)

Let
\mathrm{d}P_{t}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
be the Jacobian of the vector map

\mathbf{x}\mapsto P_{t}(\mathbf{x})
. By (
98
) of Lemma
4.2
applied
with
f=e_{j}
for each
j=1,\ldots,d
, we have

\mathrm{d}P_{t}(\mathbf{x})=\langle\mathbf{V}^{t}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}

(128)

where (with slight extension of the notation)
we write
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}
for
the average over
\{\bm{\theta}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
solving
(
97
) with initial condition
(\bm{\theta}^{0},\mathbf{V}^{0})=(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{I})
.
For
t\geq 1
, let us write also
\nabla P_{t}e_{j}(\mathbf{x})=\nabla P_{1}f(\mathbf{x})
with

f=P_{t-1}e_{j}
. Noting that
f\in\mathcal{A}

by (
96
),
we may apply (
99
) of Lemma
4.2
with this
f
.
Doing so for each
j=1,\ldots,d
gives

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}P_{t}(\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg\langle P_{t-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}(\mathbf{V}^{s})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}\bigg)^{\top}\bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\text{ for }t\geq 1.

(129)

In particular,

\sum_{j=1}^{d}(\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j})[\mathbf{x}]=\langle\Tr\mathbf{V}^{\tau}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg\langle P_{\tau-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}(\mathbf{V}^{s})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}\bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}

(130)

with the second equality holding for
\tau\geq 1
.

Now let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t},\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the solutions to (
97
)
with initial conditions
(\bm{\theta}^{0},\mathbf{V}^{0})=(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{I})
and

(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{0},\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{0})=(\tilde{\mathbf{x}},\mathbf{I})
,
coupled by the same Brownian motion
\{\mathbf{b}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
,
and write
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}
for the associated
average over
\{\bm{\theta}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{t},\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t},\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

conditional on these initial conditions.
By the form of (
97
) and by (
108
),

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\mathbf{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\nabla^{2}\log q(\bm{\theta}^{t})\cdot\mathbf{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\|\mathbf{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, so

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq e^{Ct}\|\mathbf{V}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=e^{Ct}.

(131)

Then also

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\mathbf{V}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|[\nabla^{2}\log q(\bm{\theta}^{t})-\nabla^{2}\log q(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t})]\mathbf{V}^{t}\|_{F}+\|[\nabla^{2}\log q(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t})](\mathbf{V}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{t})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla^{2}\log q(\bm{\theta}^{t})-\nabla^{2}\log q(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t})\|_{F}\|\mathbf{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|\nabla^{2}\log q(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\mathbf{V}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\|_{F}.

Applying
\nabla^{2}\log q(\bm{\theta}^{t})-\nabla^{2}\log q(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t})=\diag((\log q)^{\prime\prime}(\theta^{t}_{j})-(\log q)^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\theta}^{t}_{j}))
,
the bound
\|\nabla^{2}\log q(\bm{\theta})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C
from
(
108
),
|(\log g)^{\prime\prime\prime}(\theta)|\leq C
under Assumption

2.2
(a), and (
112
),

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\mathbf{V}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\|_{F}\leq C\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+C\|\mathbf{V}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\|_{F}\leq Ce^{Ct}\|\mathbf{x}-\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}\cdot e^{Ct}+C\|\mathbf{V}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\|_{F}.

Integrating this bound,

\|\mathbf{V}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\|_{F}\leq C\|\mathbf{x}-\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}\text{ for all }t\in[0,1].

So it follows from the first equality of (
130
)
that for
\tau\in[0,1]
,

\left|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j}(\mathbf{x})-\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})\right|=\Big|\big\langle\Tr(\mathbf{V}^{\tau}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\tau})\big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\Big|\leq\sqrt{d}\,\big\langle\|\mathbf{V}^{\tau}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\tau}\|_{F}\big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\leq C\sqrt{d}\|\mathbf{x}-\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}.

Hence by (
127
) and (
121
), for
\tau\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle|\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)-\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)|\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bm{\varphi}^{s}\|_{2}\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\leq C^{\prime}de^{-cs}.

(132)

For
\tau\geq 1
, we apply instead the second equality of (
130
) and Cauchy-Schwarz to obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j}(\mathbf{x})-\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\bigg\langle P_{\tau-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}(\mathbf{V}^{s})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}-P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{s})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}\bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\langle\Big\|P_{\tau-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})-P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}^{1/2}\bigg\langle\bigg\|\int_{0}^{1}(\mathbf{V}^{s})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}\bigg\|_{2}^{2}\bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}^{1/2}+\Big\langle\Big\|P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}^{1/2}\bigg\langle\bigg\|\int_{0}^{1}(\mathbf{V}^{s}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{s})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}\bigg\|_{2}^{2}\bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\Big\|P_{\tau-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})-P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}^{1/2}\bigg\langle\int_{0}^{1}\|\mathbf{V}^{s}\|_{F}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}^{1/2}+\Big\langle\Big\|P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}^{1/2}\bigg\langle\int_{0}^{1}\|\mathbf{V}^{s}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{s}\|_{F}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{d}\Big\langle\Big\|P_{\tau-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})-P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}^{1/2}+C\|\mathbf{x}-\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}\Big\langle\Big\|P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}^{1/2}.

(133)

Note that
\langle\|P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\leq\langle\|\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\tau}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\leq C(\|\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}^{2}+d)
by (
121
).
Applying (
122
–
123
),
(
112
), and (
121
),

\displaystyle\Big\langle\Big\|P_{\tau-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})-P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{2C(\tau-1)}\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{1}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\leq Ce^{2C\tau}\|\mathbf{x}-\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}^{2}\text{ for all }\tau\geq 1,

\displaystyle\Big\langle\Big\|P_{\tau-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})-P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-c(\tau-1)}(\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{1}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}+\langle\|\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}+d)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}e^{-c\tau}(\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\|_{2}^{2}+d)\text{ for all }\tau\geq 2.

Choosing a large enough constant
s_{0}>0
, if
\tau\in[1,s/s_{0}]
, then we may
apply the former bound, (
121
), and Cauchy-Schwarz
to (
133
) to get

\displaystyle|\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)-\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\bigg|\bigg\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C\tau}\sqrt{d}\,\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bm{\varphi}^{s}\|_{2}\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}+C\,\Big\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bm{\varphi}^{s}\|_{2}(\|\bm{\varphi}^{s}\|_{2}+\sqrt{d})\Big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}d(e^{C\tau}+1)e^{-cs}\leq C^{\prime\prime}de^{-c^{\prime}s}.

(134)

If
\tau\geq s/s_{0}
, applying instead the latter bound to
(
133
),

\displaystyle|\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)-\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-c\tau}\sqrt{d}\,\big(\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{s}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}+\langle\|\bm{\varphi}^{s}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle+d\big)^{1/2}+C\,\Big\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bm{\varphi}^{s}\|_{2}(\|\bm{\varphi}^{s}\|_{2}+\sqrt{d})\Big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}de^{-c^{\prime}s}.

(135)

Combining these bounds for
\tau\in[0,1]
,
\tau\in[1,s/s_{0}]
, and

\tau\geq s/s_{0}
in (
132
), (
134
),
and (
135
) shows (
117
).

The arguments for
\mathbf{C}_{\eta}
and
\mathbf{R}_{\eta}
in
(
118
–
119
) are similar:
For (
118
), recall the definitions (
100
)
and note that

\displaystyle\Big|\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\eta}(s+\tau,s)-\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\eta}^{\infty}(\tau)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big\langle\Big|x_{i}(\bm{\theta}^{s})P_{\tau}x_{i}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-x_{i}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})P_{\tau}x_{i}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\Big|\Big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big\langle\Big|\big(x_{i}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-x_{i}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\big)P_{\tau}x_{i}(\bm{\theta}^{s})\Big|\Big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}+\Big\langle\Big|x_{i}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\big(P_{\tau}x_{i}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-P_{\tau}x_{i}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\big)\Big|\Big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{4}}\Big\langle\lVert\mathbf{X}(\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bm{\varphi}^{s})\rVert_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}^{1/2}\Big\langle\lVert\mathbf{X}P_{\tau}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-\mathbf{y}\rVert_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}^{1/2}+\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{4}}\Big\langle\lVert\mathbf{X}\bm{\varphi}^{s}-\mathbf{y}\rVert_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle^{1/2}\Big\langle\lVert\mathbf{X}P_{\tau}(\bm{\theta}^{s})-\mathbf{X}P_{\tau}(\bm{\varphi}^{s})\rVert_{2}^{2}\Big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}^{1/2}.

The desired result (
118
) follows from
the conditions
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
,

\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{0}d
, and the preceding bounds for
(
126
).

For (
119
), note that

\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(s+\tau,s)=\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{4}}\big\langle\Tr\mathbf{X}[\mathrm{d}P_{\tau}(\bm{\theta}^{s})]\mathbf{X}^{\top}\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}},\qquad\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}^{\infty}(\tau)=\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{4}}\big\langle\Tr\mathbf{X}[\mathrm{d}P_{\tau}(\bm{\theta})]\mathbf{X}^{\top}\big\rangle.

(136)

Let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and
\{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t},\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the solutions of (
97
) with initial conditions

(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{I})
and
(\tilde{\mathbf{x}},\mathbf{I})
. If
\tau\in[0,1]
, we apply
(
128
) to obtain

|\Tr\mathbf{X}[\mathrm{d}P_{\tau}(\mathbf{x})]\mathbf{X}^{\top}-\Tr\mathbf{X}[\mathrm{d}P_{\tau}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})]\mathbf{X}^{\top}|\leq\big\langle\lVert\mathbf{V}^{\tau}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\tau}\rVert_{F}\big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\cdot\lVert\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{d}\,\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\cdot\big\langle\lVert\mathbf{V}^{\tau}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\tau}\rVert_{F}\big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}},

which leads to the bound (
132
) up to a different constant
depending on the bound
C_{0}
for
\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
. If
\tau\geq 1
, we apply
(
129
) to obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\,\Big|\Tr\mathbf{X}[\mathrm{d}P_{\tau}(\mathbf{x})]\mathbf{X}^{\top}-\Tr\mathbf{X}[\mathrm{d}P_{\tau}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})]\mathbf{X}^{\top}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\langle\Big|\Big(P_{\tau-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})-P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\Big)^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}{\mathbf{V}^{s}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}\Big)\Big|\Big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}+\Big\langle\Big|P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}(\mathbf{V}^{s}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{s})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}\Big)\Big|\Big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bigg[\Big\langle\lVert P_{\tau-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})-P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\rVert^{2}\Big\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}^{1/2}\bigg\langle\int_{0}^{1}\|\mathbf{V}^{s}\|_{F}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}^{1/2}+\Big\langle\lVert P_{\tau-1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{1})\rVert^{2}\Big\rangle_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}^{1/2}\bigg\langle\int_{0}^{1}\|\mathbf{V}^{s}-\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^{s}\|_{F}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}^{1/2}\bigg].

This can be bounded in the same way as (
133
),
(
134
), and (
135
)
up to different constants depending on the bound
C_{0}
for
\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
.
This shows (
119
).
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Suppose Assumption
2.2
(a) holds.
Let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the solution to
(
7
).
Then on the event
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})\cap\{\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{0}d\}
,
there exist constants
C,c>0
such that for all
t\geq s\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\big|\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,s)-P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})^{\top}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq Cde^{-c(t-s)}

(137)

\displaystyle|\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)|

\displaystyle\leq Cde^{-c(t-s)}

(138)

\displaystyle\bigg|\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\eta}(t,s)-\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{4}}(\mathbf{X}P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})-\mathbf{y})^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq Cde^{-c(t-s)}

(139)

\displaystyle|\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(t,s)|

\displaystyle\leq Cde^{-c(t-s)}

(140)

and furthermore

\displaystyle\big|P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})^{\top}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\leq Cde^{-cs}

(141)

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbf{X}P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})-\mathbf{y})^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y})-\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\leq Cde^{-cs}

(142)

Proof.

For (
137
) and (
141
), observe that

\displaystyle\big|\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)-P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})^{\top}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\big\langle\bm{\theta}^{s\top}(P_{\tau}\bm{\theta}^{s}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle)\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{s}\|_{2}^{2}\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}^{1/2}\big\langle\|P_{\tau}\bm{\theta}^{s}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\big\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}^{1/2}\leq Cde^{-c\tau},

the last inequality applying (
121
) and
(
124
). Similarly

\big|P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})^{\top}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\big|=\big|(P_{s}\bm{\theta}^{0}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle)^{\top}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\big|\leq\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}\cdot\|P_{s}\bm{\theta}^{0}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}\leq Cde^{-cs}.

For (
138
), recall from (
127
) that

\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle(\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j})[\bm{\theta}^{s}]\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}
.
Then by the first equality of (
130
) and (
131
),
we have
|\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)|\leq Cd
for any
\tau\in[0,1]
.
For
\tau\geq 1
, we apply instead the second equality of (
130
) where

\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbf{V}^{s}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}
is a martingale. Then

\langle\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}{\mathbf{V}^{s}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}=0

for any initial condition
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, so for any
\tau\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j}(\mathbf{x})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\bigg\langle\Big(P_{\tau-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\Big)^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}{\mathbf{V}^{s}}^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}\bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\big\langle\lVert P_{\tau-1}(\bm{\theta}^{1})-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\rVert^{2}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}^{1/2}\bigg\langle\int_{0}^{1}\|\mathbf{V}^{s}\|_{F}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}^{1/2}\leq Ce^{-c\tau}\sqrt{d}(\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}+\sqrt{d}),

the last inequality
using the estimates (
124
) and (
131
). Then
by (
121
) and Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle|\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)|\leq\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{d}(\partial_{j}P_{\tau}e_{j})[\bm{\theta}^{s}]\bigg|\bigg\rangle_{\bm{\theta}^{0}}\leq C^{\prime}de^{-c^{\prime}\tau}.

Combining these cases
\tau\in[0,1]
and
\tau\geq 1
gives
(
138
).

The arguments for (
139
), (
140
), and
(
142
), are analogous to the above, and we omit these
for brevity.
∎

4.4
The DMFT system is approximately-TTI

We now prove Theorem
2.9
, that under the log-Sobolev
condition of Assumption
2.7
(a), the DMFT system of Theorem

2.3
(a) is approximately-TTI in the
sense of Definition
2.4
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Under Assumptions
2.1
,
2.2
(a), and

2.7
(a), the DMFT
system prescribed by Theorem
2.3
(a)
satisfies the conditions of Definition
2.4
(1)
with
\varepsilon(t)=Ce^{-ct}
for some constants
C,c>0
.

Proof.

We restrict to the almost sure event where the
convergence statements of Theorem
4.3
hold, and where

\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})\cap\{\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{0}d\}
holds
for all large
n,d
.

Consider first the statements for
C_{\theta}(t,s)
. Applying

\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{0}d
and
(
137
) of Lemma
4.8
,
for some constants
C,c>0
,

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}\big|d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,s)-d^{-1}P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})^{\top}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\big|\leq Ce^{-ct}\text{ for all }s\leq t/2.

(143)

By Theorem
4.3
,

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,s)=C_{\theta}(t,s)
for all

t\geq s\geq 0
. Then, for each
s\geq 0
and
t\geq 2s
,

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})^{\top}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\leq C_{\theta}(t,s)+Ce^{-ct},\qquad\liminf_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})^{\top}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\geq C_{\theta}(t,s)-Ce^{-ct}.

Taking
t\to\infty
on the right side of both statements
shows that for each
s\geq 0
, there exists a limit

\tilde{c}_{\theta}(s):=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})^{\top}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle=\lim_{t\to\infty}C_{\theta}(t,s).

(144)

Next, (
141
) of Lemma
4.8
implies
for some
C,c>0
,

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}\big|d^{-1}P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})^{\top}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-d^{-1}\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\big|\leq Ce^{-cs}\text{ for all }s\geq 0.

(145)

Then

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\leq\tilde{c}_{\theta}(s)+Ce^{-cs},\qquad\liminf_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\geq\tilde{c}_{\theta}(s)-Ce^{-cs}.

Taking
s\to\infty
on the right side of both statements shows that there
exists a limit

c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty):=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}=\lim_{s\to\infty}\tilde{c}_{\theta}(s).

(146)

Now consider
\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)
as defined in Lemma
4.7
.
Let
{-}\mathrm{L}=\int_{0}^{\infty}a\,\mathrm{d}E_{a}
be the spectral decomposition of
{-}\mathrm{L}

as a positive, self-adjoint operator on
L^{2}(q)

(c.f.
[
86
, Theorem A.4.2]
), where
\{E_{a}\}_{a\geq 0}
is a
family of orthogonal projections onto an increasing family of closed linear
subspaces of
L^{2}(q)
. In particular,
E_{0}f=\langle f(\bm{\theta})\rangle

is the projection onto the constant functions.
For each
\tau\geq 0
and all
f,g\in L^{2}(q)
, we then have

\langle f(\bm{\theta})P_{\tau}g(\bm{\theta})\rangle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-a\tau}\mathrm{d}\langle f(\bm{\theta})E_{a}g(\bm{\theta})\rangle

(147)

understood as a Stieltjes integral with respect to the bounded-variation
function
a\mapsto\langle f(\bm{\theta})E_{a}g(\bm{\theta})\rangle

(c.f.
[
86
, Proposition 3.1.6(iii)]
).
The LSI on the event
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
implies a
spectral gap, i.e. the spectrum of
{-}\mathrm{L}
is
included in
\{0\}\cup[1/C_{\mathrm{LSI}},\infty)
.
Thus, fixing any constant
\iota\in(0,1/C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
, we have

\displaystyle d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)=d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle e_{j}(\bm{\theta})P_{\tau}e_{j}(\bm{\theta})\rangle

\displaystyle=d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle e_{j}(\bm{\theta})E_{0}e_{j}(\bm{\theta})\rangle+d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\int_{\iota}^{\infty}e^{-a\tau}\mathrm{d}\langle e_{j}(\bm{\theta})E_{a}e_{j}(\bm{\theta})\rangle

\displaystyle=d^{-1}\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}+d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\int_{\iota}^{\infty}e^{-a\tau}\mathrm{d}\langle e_{j}(\bm{\theta})E_{a}e_{j}(\bm{\theta})\rangle,

the first equality applying (
147
), and
the second equality applying
\sum_{j}\langle e_{j}(\bm{\theta})E_{0}e_{j}(\bm{\theta})\rangle=\sum_{j}\langle\theta_{j}\langle\theta_{j}\rangle\rangle=\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|^{2}
.
Define
(n,d,\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})
-dependent scalars
c_{\theta,d},m_{\theta,d}>0

and a positive measure
\mu_{\theta,d}
on
[\iota,\infty)
by

c_{\theta,d}=d^{-1}\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2},\quad m_{\theta,d}=d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\int_{\iota}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\langle e_{j}(\bm{\theta})E_{a}e_{j}(\bm{\theta})\rangle,\quad\mu_{\theta,d}(S)=d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\int_{S}\mathrm{d}\langle e_{j}(\bm{\theta})E_{a}e_{j}(\bm{\theta})\rangle,

(148)

noting that
a\mapsto d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle e_{j}(\bm{\theta})E_{a}e_{j}(\bm{\theta})\rangle
is nondecreasing and hence
defines a valid distribution function for
\mu_{\theta,d}
. Then

d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)=c_{\theta,d}+\int_{\iota}^{\infty}e^{-a\tau}\mu_{\theta,d}(\mathrm{d}a),\qquad m_{\theta,d}=\mu_{\theta,d}([\iota,\infty)).

(149)

Applying (
149
) with
\tau=0
,

c_{\theta,d}+m_{\theta,d}=d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(0)=d^{-1}\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq C.

(150)

In particular,
\mu_{\theta,d}
is finite and uniformly bounded in total
variation norm for all
(n,d)
.
We claim that
\tau\mapsto d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)
is uniformly
equicontinuous over all
(n,d)
: Observe that

\displaystyle\Big|d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)-d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau^{\prime})\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|d^{-1}\big\langle\bm{\theta}^{\top}[P_{\tau}-P_{\tau^{\prime}}](\bm{\theta})\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\leq d^{-1}\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle^{1/2}\cdot\langle\|[P_{\tau}-P_{\tau^{\prime}}](\bm{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle^{1/2}

\displaystyle=d^{-1}\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle^{1/2}\cdot\langle\|\bm{\theta}-P_{|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|}(\bm{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle^{1/2}.

(151)

[
87
, Theorem II.2.1]
implies

\|P_{t}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C(1+\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2})t
for
all
t\in[0,1]
and a constant
C>0
.
This and (
121
) imply
that the right side of (
151
) is at most

C^{\prime}|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|
for a constant
C^{\prime}>0
and all
|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|\leq 1
,
so
\tau\mapsto d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)
is uniformly
equicontinuous as claimed. We note that for any
M>0
, by the relation
(
149
),

c_{\theta,d}+\mu_{\theta,d}([0,M))+e^{-M\tau}\mu_{\theta,d}([M,\infty))\geq d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)
. Then setting
\tau=1/M
and rearranging
yields

(1-e^{-1})\mu_{\theta,d}([M,\infty))\leq c_{\theta,d}+m_{\theta,d}-d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(1/M)=d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(0)-d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(1/M).

So this uniform equicontinuity implies
that the measures
\mu_{\theta,d}
are uniformly tight.

Then, there exists a subsequence
\{(n_{k},d_{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
of
(n,d)
along
which
\mu_{\theta,d}\Rightarrow\mu_{\theta}
weakly, for some finite
positive measure
\mu_{\theta}
on
[\iota,\infty)
. Recalling also that

c_{\theta,d}=d^{-1}\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\to c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
as
n,d\to\infty

by the definition (
146
), and setting

c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)=c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)+\int_{\iota}^{\infty}e^{-a\tau}\mu_{\theta}(\mathrm{d}a),

(152)

this weak convergence applied to (
149
) implies

\lim_{k\to\infty}d_{k}^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)=c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)
.
Combining this with the convergence

\lim_{k\to\infty}d_{k}^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)=C_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)

by Theorem
4.3
, for any
s,\tau\geq 0
we have

\Big|C_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)\Big|\leq\limsup_{k\to\infty}\Big|d_{k}^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)-d_{k}^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)\Big|\leq Ce^{-cs},

(153)

where the last inequality holds by (
116
).
Since
C_{\theta}(t,s)
is non-random,
this implies that
c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)
is also non-random for every

\tau\geq 0
, and thus also the measure
\mu_{\theta}
is non-random. This shows (
30
).

The statement (
31
) follows analogously:
By arguments parallel to (
144
) and (
146
), applying
Theorem
4.3
and (
139
) and
(
142
) shows that there exist limits

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{\eta}(s)

\displaystyle:=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{\delta}{n\sigma^{4}}(\mathbf{X}P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})-\mathbf{y})^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y})=\lim_{t\to\infty}C_{\eta}(t,s),

(154)

\displaystyle c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)

\displaystyle:=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{\delta}{n\sigma^{4}}\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}=\lim_{s\to\infty}\tilde{c}_{\eta}(s).

(155)

Note that

n^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\eta}^{\infty}(\tau)=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\langle x_{i}(\bm{\theta})P_{\tau}x_{i}(\bm{\theta})\big\rangle=\frac{\delta}{n\sigma^{4}}\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\iota}^{\infty}e^{-a\tau}\mathrm{d}\langle x_{i}(\bm{\theta})E_{a}x_{i}(\bm{\theta})\rangle.

Defining

c_{\eta,n}=\frac{\delta}{n\sigma^{4}}\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2},\quad\mu_{\eta,n}(S)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{S}\mathrm{d}\langle x_{i}(\bm{\theta})E_{a}x_{i}(\bm{\theta})\rangle,\quad m_{\eta,n}=\mu_{\eta,n}([\iota,\infty)),

(156)

we have

c_{\eta,n}+m_{\eta,n}=n^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\eta}^{\infty}(0)=\frac{\delta}{n\sigma^{4}}\langle\|\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq C.

(157)

So along some subsequence
\{(n_{k},d_{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
, we have

c_{\eta,n}\to c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
,
\mu_{\eta,n}\Rightarrow\mu_{\eta}

weakly for a finite positive measure
\mu_{\eta}
on
[\iota,\infty)
, and

\lim_{k\to\infty}n_{k}^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\eta}^{\infty}(\tau)=c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)
for the quantity

c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)=c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)+\int_{\iota}^{\infty}e^{-a\tau}\mu_{\eta}(\mathrm{d}a).

By an argument parallel to (
153
) using Theorem
4.3
and (
118
), this shows

|C_{\eta}(s+\tau,s)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)|\leq Ce^{-cs}
, establishing (
31
).

Finally, for (
32
), observe that by Theorem

4.3
,
\lim_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*}=C_{\theta}(s,*)
. Noting that

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\big|(P_{s}(\bm{\theta}^{0})-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle)^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*}\big|\leq\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\|P_{s}\bm{\theta}^{0}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}\cdot\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}\leq Ce^{-cs}

by (
124
), this implies the existence of the limit

c_{\theta}(*):=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*}=\lim_{s\to\infty}C_{\theta}(s,*),

(158)

which satisfies
|C_{\theta}(s,*)-c_{\theta}(*)|\leq Ce^{-cs}
.
This shows (
32
).
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Under Assumptions
2.1
,
2.2
(a), and

2.7
(a), the DMFT
system prescribed by Theorem
2.3
(a)
satisfies the conditions of Definition
2.4
(2)
with
\varepsilon(t)=Ce^{-ct}
for some constants
C,c>0
.

Proof.

We again restrict to the almost sure event where the convergence statements of
Theorem
4.3
hold,
and where
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})\cap\{\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{0}d\}
holds
for all large
n,d
.

Consider first
R_{\theta}(t,s)
. By
(
138
) of Lemma
4.8
and the convergence

R_{\theta}(t,s)=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)
of Theorem

4.3
,

|R_{\theta}(t,s)|\leq Ce^{-ct}\text{ for all }s\leq t/2.

(159)

For
s\geq t/2
, note that
the forms of (
149
) and (
152
) imply that both

d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)
and
c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)

are convex and differentiable in
\tau\geq 0
. Then,
along the subsequence
\{(n_{k},d_{k})\}_{k\geq 1}

of the preceding proof, the pointwise convergence

\lim_{k\to\infty}d_{k}^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)=c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)
implies also

\lim_{k\to\infty}d_{k}^{-1}\partial_{\tau}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)=\partial_{\tau}c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)

for each
\tau\geq 0
(c.f.
[
88
, Theorem 25.7]
). By
the fluctuation-dissipation relation of Lemma
4.1

applied with
A=B=e_{j}
for each
j=1,\ldots,d
, we have

\partial_{\tau}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)={-}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)
.
Then, defining
r_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)={-}\partial_{\tau}c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)
,
this shows
\lim_{k\to\infty}d_{k}^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)=r_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)
. Combining with

\lim_{k\to\infty}d_{k}^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)=R_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)

from Theorem
4.3
, for any
s,\tau\geq 0
we have that

\big|R_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)-r_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)\big|\leq\limsup_{k\to\infty}\Big|d_{k}^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(s+\tau,s)-d_{k}^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\infty}(\tau)\Big|\leq Ce^{-cs},

where the last inequality applies (
117
).
In particular, for any
t\geq 0
,

|R_{\theta}(t,s)-r_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(t-s)|\leq Ce^{-c^{\prime}t}\text{ for all }s\in[t/2,t].

(160)

Together, (
159
) and (
160
)
imply (
34
).
The statement (
35
) follows analogously, and we omit
this for brevity.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.9
.

This follows from Lemmas
4.9
and
4.10
.
∎

4.5
Limit MSE and free energy

We now show Corollary
2.10
on the asymptotic
values of the mean-squared-errors and the free energy.

Proposition 4.11
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.1
,
2.2
(a), and

2.7
(a) hold. Let
\YMSE_{*}
and the marginal likelihood

\mathsf{P}_{g}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})
be as defined in Corollary
2.10
.
Let
\mathbb{E}[\cdot\mid\mathbf{X}]
denote the expectation with respect to
\theta_{j}^{*}\overset{iid}{\sim}g_{*}
and
\varepsilon_{i}\overset{iid}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})

conditioning on
\mathbf{X}
. Then almost surely,

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\log\mathsf{P}_{g}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})-d^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\log\mathsf{P}_{g}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})\mid\mathbf{X}]=0,\qquad\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\YMSE_{*}-\mathbb{E}[\YMSE_{*}\mid\mathbf{X}]=0.

(161)

Proof.

We condition on
\mathbf{X}
throughout, and restrict to the
\mathbf{X}
-dependent event

\{\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}\text{ and }(\ref{eq:LSI})\text{ holds}\}.

Note that by assumption, this event holds a.s. for all large
n,d
and
does not depend on
\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}
.

For the first statement, let us consider

\displaystyle Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})=\log\int\exp\bigg({-}\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\|\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\bm{\varepsilon}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\log g(\theta_{j})\bigg)\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}

(which coincides with
\log P_{g}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})
up to an additive constant)
as a function of
(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
. Then

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{*}}Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})={-}\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\bm{\varepsilon}-\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle),\qquad\nabla_{\bm{\varepsilon}}Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})={-}\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\bm{\varepsilon}-\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle).

Under Assumption
2.1
, note that

\bm{\theta}^{*}
and
\bm{\varepsilon}
have independent
subgaussian entries, so there are constants
C_{1},c>0
such that
(c.f.
[
84
, Eq. (3.1)]
)

\mathbb{P}[\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}>C_{1}d]\leq e^{-cd}.

(162)

When
\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{1}d
, we have the bound

\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq Cd
from (
106
).
Applying this and
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
,

\|\nabla_{(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})}Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\|_{2}\mathbf{1}\{\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{1}d\}\leq L\sqrt{d}

for a constant
L>0
. Thus
Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
is
L\sqrt{d}
-Lipschitz on

\{\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{1}d\}
, so its Lipschitz extension

\tilde{Z}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})=\inf_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+n}:\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{1}d}Z(\mathbf{x})+L\sqrt{d}\|\mathbf{x}-(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\|_{2}

is globally
L\sqrt{d}
-Lipschitz on
\mathbb{R}^{d+n}

and
\tilde{Z}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})=Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
over

\{\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{1}d\}
.
Under Assumption
2.1
, the joint distribution of

(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
satisfies a log-Sobolev inequality by tensorization,
implying the Lipschitz concentration

\mathbb{P}[|\tilde{Z}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Z}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{X}]|\geq td\mid\mathbf{X}]\leq 2e^{-t^{2}d/(2L^{2})}.

(163)

We may bound

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Z}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{X}]-\mathbb{E}[Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{X}]|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\geq C_{1}d\}\Big(|\tilde{Z}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})|+|Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})|\Big)\;\Big|\;\mathbf{X}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\geq C_{1}d\mid\mathbf{X}]^{1/2}\Big((\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Z}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{X}]^{2})^{1/2}+(\mathbb{E}[Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{X}]^{2})^{1/2}\Big)

Applying the upper bound
Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\leq\log\int\exp(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\log g(\theta_{j}))\mathrm{d}\bm{\theta}=0
, Jensen’s inequality lower bound

Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\geq\mathbb{E}_{g}[-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\|\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\bm{\varepsilon}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}]

where
\mathbb{E}_{g}[\cdot]
is the expectation over
\theta_{j}\overset{iid}{\sim}g
,
and
|\tilde{Z}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-Z(0)|=|\tilde{Z}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-\tilde{Z}(0)|\leq L\sqrt{d}(\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2})^{1/2}
, we obtain

|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Z}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{X}]-\mathbb{E}[Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{X}]|\leq\mathbb{P}[\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\geq C_{1}d\mid\mathbf{X}]^{1/2}\cdot Cd\leq e^{-c^{\prime}d}

for all large
n,d
, the last inequality applying (
162
).
Thus (
163
) and (
162
) imply

\mathbb{P}[|Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})-\mathbb{E}[Z(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{X}]|\geq td+e^{-c^{\prime}d}\mid\mathbf{X}]\leq 2e^{-t^{2}d/(2L^{2})}+e^{-cd},

implying the first statement of (
161
) by the
Borel-Cantelli lemma.

For the second statement, let us write

n\YMSE_{*}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})=\|\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\bm{\varepsilon}-\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}

viewed also as a function of
(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
. Writing
\kappa_{2}(\cdot)
for
the covariance associated to the posterior mean
\langle\cdot\rangle
,
differentiating in
(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
gives, for any unit vectors
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

and
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{*}}[n\YMSE_{*}]

\displaystyle=2(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\bm{\varepsilon}-\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle)^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{u}-\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}}\kappa_{2}\Big(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{u},(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\bm{\varepsilon}-\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle)^{\top}\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}\Big),

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{\varepsilon}}[n\YMSE_{*}]

\displaystyle=2(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\bm{\varepsilon}-\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle)^{\top}\mathbf{v}-\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}}\kappa_{2}\Big(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{v},(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\bm{\varepsilon}-\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle)^{\top}\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}\Big).

The Poincaré inequality implied by the assumed LSI for

\mathsf{P}_{g}(\bm{\theta}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})
shows, for any vector
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\kappa_{2}(\mathbf{x}^{\top}\bm{\theta},\mathbf{u}^{\top}\bm{\theta})\leq C\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}.

On the event
\{\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{1}d\}
,
applying this Poincaré bound, Cauchy-Schwarz for
\kappa_{2}(\cdot)
,
and
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
and
\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq Cd
from
(
106
), we obtain

|\mathbf{u}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{*}}[n\YMSE_{*}]|\leq C\sqrt{d}
and

|\mathbf{v}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{\varepsilon}}[n\YMSE_{*}]|\leq C\sqrt{d}
for any unit vectors

\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}
, and hence

\|\nabla_{\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}}[n\YMSE_{*}]\|_{2}\mathbf{1}\{\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{1}d\}\leq L\sqrt{d}

for some constant
L>0
. So
n\YMSE_{*}
is

L\sqrt{d}
-Lipschitz in
(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})

on
\{\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{1}d\}
.
For any
(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
, we also have the bound

|n\YMSE_{*}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})|\leq C(\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2})^{1/2}

by (
106
), so the second statement of
(
161
) follows from the same Lipschitz extension and
concentration argument as above.
∎

Proof of Corollary
2.10
(a).

We restrict to the almost sure event where
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
holds
for all large
n,d
.
Observe that by (
148
) and (
150
),

\MSE=d^{-1}\langle\|\bm{\theta}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\rangle=d^{-1}\big(\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle-\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\big)=m_{d}=\mu_{d}([\iota,\infty)),

so
\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\MSE=\mu_{\theta}([\iota,\infty))=c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)

by (
152
). Also

\MSE_{*}=d^{-1}\|\bm{\theta}^{*}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}=d^{-1}\big(\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-2\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\big),

so

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\MSE_{*}=\mathbb{E}[{\theta^{*}}^{2}]-2c_{\theta}(*)+c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
by Assumption
2.1
and the
definitions (
146
) and (
158
).
Thus
\MSE\to\mse
and
\MSE_{*}\to\mse_{*}
for the quantities

\mse,\mse_{*}
defined in (
42
).

Similarly

\YMSE=n^{-1}\big\langle\|\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}-\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\big\rangle=n^{-1}\big(\langle\|\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle-\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}\big).

Then by (
156
) and (
157
),

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}n^{-1}\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\delta}c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
and

\YMSE=\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\delta}\mu_{\eta,n}([\iota,\infty))\rightarrow\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\delta}(c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))=\ymse

as defined in (
42
). For
\YMSE_{*}
,
writing
\mathbb{E}[\cdot\mid\mathbf{X}]
for the expectation over
(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
as in
Proposition
4.11
, observe first that

\displaystyle n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]-2n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\bm{\varepsilon}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*})+\sigma^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}],

and Gaussian integration-by-parts gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\bm{\varepsilon}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*})\mid\mathbf{X}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\bm{\varepsilon}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\mid\mathbf{X}]=\mathbb{E}[\langle\|\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\mid\mathbf{X}]-\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\langle\|\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}-\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\mid\mathbf{X}]=n\,\mathbb{E}[\YMSE\mid\mathbf{X}].

Thus

\mathbb{E}[\YMSE_{*}\mid\mathbf{X}]=n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]=n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]+2\mathbb{E}[\YMSE\mid\mathbf{X}]-\sigma^{2}.

(164)

We remark that
n^{-1}\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\YMSE
are
bounded for all large
n,d
on the event
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
, by the
bound for
\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq C
from (
106
).
Thus, applying
\YMSE\to\ymse
and

n^{-1}\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}\to\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\delta}c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)
as argued above and dominated
convergence, the right side of (
164
) converges to

\ymse_{*}=\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\delta}(2c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(0)-c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty))-\sigma^{2}

as defined in (
42
). Then the concentration of
\YMSE_{*}
established
in Proposition
4.11
combined with (
164
)
show
\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\YMSE_{*}=\ymse_{*}
.

To show the last statement (
48
), conditional on

\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}
and averaging over the initial condition
\bm{\theta}^{0}\sim q_{0}=g_{0}^{\otimes d}
, let
q_{t}
be the conditional law of
\bm{\theta}^{t}
.
Consider a coupling of a posterior sample

\bm{\theta}\sim q
with
\bm{\theta}^{t}\sim q_{t}
such that

\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle=W_{2}(q_{t},q)^{2}
, where
\langle\cdot\rangle
denotes the expectation under this coupling and
W_{2}(\cdot)
is the
Wasserstein-2 distance, both conditional on
\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}
.
For a given realization of
(\bm{\theta}^{t},\bm{\theta})
from this coupling,
considering the
coordinatewise coupling of
\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j}^{t})}
with

\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j})}
shows

W_{2}\left(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j}^{t})},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j})}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(\theta_{j}^{t}-\theta_{j})^{2}=\frac{1}{d}\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}.

Then

\Bigg\langle W_{2}\Bigg(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j}^{t})},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j})}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg\rangle\leq\frac{1}{d}\big\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\big\rangle=\frac{1}{d}W_{2}(q_{t},q)^{2}.

Applying Lemmas
4.4
and
4.6
,

W_{2}(q_{t},q)^{2}\leq Ce^{-ct}(\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle+\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle)\leq C^{\prime}de^{-ct}

on the event
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
,
for some constants
C,C^{\prime},c>0
. So on this event,

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}\Bigg\langle W_{2}\Bigg(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j}^{t})},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j})}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg\rangle\leq C^{\prime}e^{-ct}.

(165)

Now by Theorem
2.3
(a), for each fixed
t\geq 0
,
almost surely with respect to the randomness of both

\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}
and

\bm{\theta}^{0},\{\mathbf{b}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
defining
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
, we have

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}W_{2}\Bigg(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j}^{t})},\;\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{t})\Bigg)^{2}=0

(166)

where
\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{t})
here is the law of
(\theta^{*},\theta^{t})
in
the DMFT system. To take an expectation over the randomness of

\bm{\theta}^{0}
and
\{\mathbf{b}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
, note that
from the definition (
103
), we have

\bm{\theta}^{t}=\bm{\theta}^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{\bm{\theta}}\log q(\bm{\theta}^{s})\,\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{b}^{t}=\bm{\theta}^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s})+(\log g)^{\prime}(\bm{\theta}^{s})\Big]\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{b}^{t},

where
(\log g)^{\prime}
is applied entrywise.
Then on
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
,
by the Lipschitz continuity of
(\log g)^{\prime}(\theta)
, this implies for a constant

C>0
that

d^{-1/2}\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|_{2}\leq\int_{0}^{t}Cd^{-1/2}\|\bm{\theta}^{s}\|_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+Ct+d^{-1/2}\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2}+\sqrt{2}\,d^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{b}^{t}\|_{2}.

Then for any
T>0
, Gronwall’s inequality gives, for a constant
C>0
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d^{-1/2}\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|_{2}\leq Ce^{CT}\Big(T+d^{-1/2}\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2}+d^{-1/2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\mathbf{b}^{t}\|_{2}\Big)

(167)

For any
p>1
, applying

\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d^{-1}\|\mathbf{b}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)^{p}\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}|b_{j}^{t}|^{2p}\leq d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|b_{j}^{t}|^{2p}

and Doob’s
L^{p}
-maximal inequality, we have
that
\langle(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d^{-1}\|\mathbf{b}^{t}\|_{2}^{2})^{p}\rangle
is bounded by a

(T,p)
-dependent constant. Similarly
\langle(d^{-1}\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2})^{p}\rangle
is bounded by a
(T,p)
-dependent constant, so

\Big\langle\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d^{-1}\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)^{p}\Big\rangle\leq C_{T,p}

for a constant
C_{T,p}>0
, where
\langle\cdot\rangle
averages over

\bm{\theta}^{0}
and
\{\mathbf{b}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
.
Since
W_{2}(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j}^{t})},\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{t}))^{2}\leq C(d^{-1}\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+d^{-1}\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}+\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t})^{2})
,
this implies on the event
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
that

\bigg\langle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}W_{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j}^{t})},\;\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{t})\bigg)^{2p}\bigg\rangle\leq C_{T,p}^{\prime}

(168)

for a different constant
C_{T,p}^{\prime}>0
. In particular, for any fixed
t\geq 0

and
p>1
, the squared Wasserstein-2
distance in (
166
) is uniformly bounded in
L^{p}
and hence
uniformly integrable with respect to
\langle\cdot\rangle
for all large

n,d
, so dominated convergence implies, almost surely,

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\Bigg\langle W_{2}\Bigg(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j}^{t})},\;\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{t})\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg\rangle=0.

(169)

Combining (
165
) and
(
169
) shows that
for any fixed
t\geq 0
, almost surely,

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}\Bigg\langle W_{2}\Bigg(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{j})},\mathsf{P}_{g_{*},\omega_{*};g,\omega}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg\rangle\leq C\Big(e^{-ct}+W_{2}(\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{t}),\;\mathsf{P}_{g_{*},\omega_{*};g,\omega})^{2}\Big).

By Theorem
2.5
, we have

\lim_{t\to\infty}W_{2}(\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{t}),\;\mathsf{P}_{g_{*},\omega_{*};g,\omega})=0

so taking the limit
t\to\infty
shows (
48
).
∎

To show Corollary
2.10
(b) on the asymptotic free
energy, we will apply an I-MMSE argument, together with the following
proposition which guarantees continuity of
\mse,\mse_{*}
in the noise
variance
\sigma^{2}
.
In the later proof of Theorem
2.13
, we will require
also continuity in the prior parameter
\alpha
; thus we establish both
statements here.

Lemma 4.12
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.1
and
2.2
(b) hold.
Fix any open subset
O\subset\mathbb{R}^{K}
, and suppose also that Assumption

2.7
holds for each
g\in\{g(\cdot,\alpha):\alpha\in O\}
,
where the constant
C_{\mathrm{LSI}}>0
is uniform over
\alpha\in O
.
Consider any noise variance
\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\geq\sigma^{2}
, and define

\mse(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\alpha),\mse_{*}(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\alpha)
by
(
42
) via the (approximately-TTI) DMFT limit of the Langevin dynamics
(
7
) with a fixed prior
g(\cdot,\alpha)

in the linear model (
4
) with noise variance
\tilde{\sigma}^{2}
.

Then over any compact interval

I\subset[\sigma^{2},\infty)
and compact subset
S\subset O
,

\mse(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\alpha),\mse_{*}(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\alpha)
are Lipschitz functions of

(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\alpha)\in I\times S
.

Proof.

Consider noise/prior parameters
(s^{2},\alpha)
and

(\tilde{s}^{2},\tilde{\alpha})
, where
s^{2},\tilde{s}^{2}\geq\sigma^{2}
.
Let us couple the linear models with noise
variances
s^{2}
and
\tilde{s}^{2}
by
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+s\mathbf{z}

and
\tilde{\mathbf{y}}=\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\tilde{s}\mathbf{z}
, where
\mathbf{z}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbf{I})
.
Fixing
\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{z}
, let us denote

U(\bm{\theta})={-}\frac{1}{2s^{2}}\|\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+s\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\log g(\theta_{j},\alpha)

so that
q(\bm{\theta})\propto e^{U(\bm{\theta})}
is the posterior law given
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})

under parameters
(s^{2},\alpha)
. Denote similarly
\tilde{U}(\bm{\theta})

with
(\tilde{s}^{2},\tilde{\alpha})
in place of
(s^{2},\alpha)
,
and
\tilde{q}(\bm{\theta})\propto e^{\tilde{U}(\bm{\theta})}

as the posterior law given
(\mathbf{X},\tilde{\mathbf{y}})
.
We condition on
\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{z}
and restrict to the event

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})=\{\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0},\,\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2},\|\mathbf{z}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{0}d,\;(\ref{eq:LSI})\text{ holds for both }q\text{ and }\tilde{q}\},

which by assumption holds a.s. for all large
n,d
. We first derive a bound on
the Wasserstein-2 distance between
q
and
\tilde{q}
, conditional on

\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{z}
.

Let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the
Langevin diffusion (
103
) with fixed prior
g(\cdot,\alpha)

and stationary distribution
q(\bm{\theta})
, initialized as
\bm{\theta}^{0}\sim q_{0}

where
q_{0}
has finite second moment and finite entropy. Let us write

\langle f(\bm{\theta}^{t})\rangle
for the expectation over
\bm{\theta}^{0}
and

\{\mathbf{b}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
defining (
103
),
conditional on
\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{z}
.
We apply the following argument of

[
89
]
to bound the KL-divergence
\DKL(q_{t}\|\tilde{q})

conditional on
\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{z}
:
Differentiating this KL-divergence in time,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\DKL(q_{t}\|\tilde{q})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int q_{t}(\log q_{t}-\log\tilde{q})

\displaystyle=\int\Big(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}q_{t}\Big)(\log q_{t}-\log\tilde{q})+\underbrace{\int\frac{q_{t}}{q_{t}}\Big(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}q_{t}\Big)}_{=0}=\int\Big(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}q_{t}\Big)(\log q_{t}-\tilde{U}+\log\tilde{Z}).

The law of
\bm{\theta}^{t}
conditional on
\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{z}

admits a density
q_{t}
which is described by the Fokker-Planck equation

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}q_{t}=\nabla\cdot[q_{t}\nabla(\log q_{t}-U)]

with initial condition
q_{t}|_{t=0}=q_{0}
.
Then, applying this Fokker-Planck equation and
integrating by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\DKL(q_{t}\|\tilde{q})

\displaystyle={-}\int q_{t}\,\nabla(\log q_{t}-U)^{\top}\nabla(\log q_{t}-\tilde{U})

\displaystyle={-}\int q_{t}\,\|\nabla(\log q_{t}-\tilde{U})\|_{2}^{2}-\int q_{t}\,\nabla(\tilde{U}-U)^{\top}\nabla(\log q_{t}-\tilde{U})

\displaystyle\leq{-}(1/2)\int q_{t}\,\|\nabla(\log q_{t}-\tilde{U})\|_{2}^{2}+(1/2)\int q_{t}\|\nabla(\tilde{U}-U)\|_{2}^{2},

the last step applying Cauchy-Schwarz for the second term. By the LSI for

\tilde{q}
, the first term (the relative Fisher information) is lower bounded as

\int q_{t}\,\|\nabla(\log q_{t}-\tilde{U})\|_{2}^{2}=\int q_{t}\,\Big\|\nabla\log\frac{q_{t}}{\tilde{q}}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2C_{\mathrm{LSI}}}\,\DKL(q_{t}\|\tilde{q}).

Thus

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\DKL(q_{t}\|\tilde{q})\leq{-}\frac{1}{4C_{\mathrm{LSI}}}\,\DKL(q_{t}\|\tilde{q})+\frac{1}{2}\,\underbrace{\langle\|\nabla\tilde{U}(\bm{\theta}^{t})-\nabla U(\bm{\theta}^{t})\|_{2}^{2}\rangle}_{:=\Delta(t)}.

Integrating this inequality shows,
for some constants
C,c>0
depending only on
C_{\mathrm{LSI}}
and for any
T>0
,

\DKL(q_{T}\|\tilde{q})\leq C\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Delta(t)+e^{-cT}\DKL(q_{0}\|\tilde{q})\Big).

(170)

We now specialize (
170
) to the initialization
q_{0}=\tilde{q}
,
and bound
\Delta(t)
. We have

\Delta(t)\leq\Bigg\langle\bigg\|\frac{1}{s^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+s\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{t})-\frac{1}{\tilde{s}^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\tilde{s}\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{t})\bigg\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Big(\partial_{\theta}\log g(\theta_{j}^{t},\alpha)-\partial_{\theta}\log g(\theta_{j}^{t},\tilde{\alpha})\Big)^{2}\Bigg\rangle.

Let
C,C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}>0
be constants depending on the compact sets
S,I
of the lemma
statement and changing
from instance to instance. For
\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in S
and

s^{2},\tilde{s}^{2}\in I
,

|s^{-2}-\tilde{s}^{-2}|\leq C|s^{2}-\tilde{s}^{2}|,\quad|s^{-1}-\tilde{s}^{-1}|\leq C|s^{2}-\tilde{s}^{2}|,\quad|\partial_{\theta}\log g(\theta;\alpha)-\partial_{\theta}\log g(\theta;\tilde{\alpha})|\leq C\|\alpha-\tilde{\alpha}\|_{2},

the last inequality holding by Assumption
2.2
(b). Thus

\Delta(t)\leq C\Big[\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{4}(\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle)+\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|_{2}^{2}\Big](s^{2}-\tilde{s}^{2})^{2}+Cd\|\alpha-\tilde{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}.

On the event
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
, we have

\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq C(\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle+d)
by (
121
),
and
\langle\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle\leq Cd
under the initialization

q_{0}=\tilde{q}
which holds also by (
121
).
Applying these bounds together
with
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C
,
\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq Cd
, and

\|\mathbf{z}\|_{2}^{2}\leq Cd
by definition of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
, we have

\sup_{t\geq 0}\Delta(t)\leq C^{\prime}d(s^{2}-\tilde{s}^{2})^{2}+C^{\prime}d\|\alpha-\tilde{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}.

Applying this and
q_{0}=\tilde{q}
to (
170
),
we have on the event
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
that

\sup_{t\geq 0}\DKL(q_{t}\|\tilde{q})\leq Cd(s^{2}-\tilde{s}^{2})^{2}+Cd\|\alpha-\tilde{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}.

By lower-semicontinuity of KL-divergence and the
T_{2}
-transportation
inequality for
\tilde{q}
implied by the LSI
(c.f.
[
86
, Theorem 9.6.1]
),

W_{2}(q,\tilde{q})^{2}\leq C\DKL(q\|\tilde{q})\leq C\liminf_{t\to\infty}\DKL(q_{t}\|\tilde{q})\leq C^{\prime}d(s^{2}-\tilde{s}^{2})^{2}+C^{\prime}d\|\alpha-\tilde{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}.

(171)

This gives our desired bound on the Wasserstein-2
distance between
q
and
\tilde{q}
.

Now let
\langle f(\bm{\theta})\rangle_{q}
and
\langle f(\bm{\theta})\rangle_{\tilde{q}}
be the posterior expectations under
q
(given
\mathbf{y}
) and
\tilde{q}
(given

\tilde{\mathbf{y}}
). Then by Jensen’s inequality,

\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{q}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{\tilde{q}}\|_{2}\leq W_{2}(q,\tilde{q}).

Applying
|\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}|\leq\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{y}\|_{2}
and
Cauchy-Schwarz, also

|\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{q}-\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\tilde{q}}|\leq W_{2}(q,\tilde{q})\cdot\sqrt{2\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{q}+2\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\tilde{q}}}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,W_{2}(q,\tilde{q})

where the last inequality applies

\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}\rangle_{q}\leq\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{q}^{1/2}\leq C\sqrt{d}

on
\mathcal{E}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
by (
121
),
and similarly for
\tilde{q}
. Then, denoting by
\MSE(s^{2},\alpha)

and
\MSE(\tilde{s}^{2},\tilde{\alpha})
the values of
\MSE
as defined in
Corollary
2.10
under
q
and
\tilde{q}
, we have

\displaystyle|\MSE(s^{2},\alpha)-\MSE(\tilde{s}^{2},\tilde{\alpha})|

\displaystyle=\big|d^{-1}\langle\|\bm{\theta}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{q}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{q}-d^{-1}\langle\|\bm{\theta}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{\tilde{q}}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\tilde{q}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq d^{-1}\big|\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{q}-\langle\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle_{\tilde{q}}\big|+d^{-1}\big|\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{q}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{\tilde{q}}\|_{2}^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}W_{2}(q,\tilde{q})}{\sqrt{d}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}|s^{2}-\tilde{s}^{2}|+C^{\prime\prime}\|\alpha-\tilde{\alpha}\|_{2}.

Similarly

\displaystyle|\MSE_{*}(s^{2},\alpha)-\MSE_{*}(\tilde{s}^{2},\tilde{\alpha})|

\displaystyle=\big|d^{-1}\|\bm{\theta}^{*}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{q}\|_{2}^{2}-d^{-1}\|\bm{\theta}^{*}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{\tilde{q}}\|_{2}^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2d^{-1}\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{q}-\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{\tilde{q}}\|_{2}+d^{-1}\big|\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{q}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle_{\tilde{q}}\|_{2}^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}W_{2}(q,\tilde{q})}{\sqrt{d}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}|s^{2}-\tilde{s}^{2}|+C^{\prime\prime}\|\alpha-\tilde{\alpha}\|_{2}.

Since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(C_{0},C_{\mathrm{LSI}})
holds a.s. for all large
n,d
,
and Corollary
2.10
(a)
already proven shows
\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\MSE=\mse
and

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\MSE_{*}=\mse_{*}
a.s. at both
(s^{2},\alpha)
and
(\tilde{s}^{2},\tilde{\alpha})
, this implies

|\mse(s^{2},\alpha)-\mse(\tilde{s}^{2},\tilde{\alpha})|,|\mse_{*}(s^{2},\alpha)-\mse_{*}(\tilde{s}^{2},\tilde{\alpha})|\leq C|s^{2}-\tilde{s}^{2}|+C\|\alpha-\tilde{\alpha}\|_{2},

so
\mse(s^{2},\alpha)
and
\mse_{*}(s^{2},\alpha)
are locally Lipschitz as desired.
∎

Proof of Corollary
2.10
(b).

We apply Corollary
2.10
(a) and an
I-MMSE relation for mismatched Gaussian channels.
Write
\mathbb{E}[\cdot\mid\mathbf{X}]
for the expectation over
(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
conditional on
\mathbf{X}

as in Proposition

4.11
. Let

I(\mathbf{y},\bm{\theta}^{*})=\mathbb{E}\left[\log\frac{\mathsf{P}(\mathbf{y}\mid\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{X})}{\mathsf{P}_{g_{*}}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})}\;\bigg|\;\mathbf{X}\right]={-}\mathbb{E}[\log\mathsf{P}_{g_{*}}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})\mid\mathbf{X}]-\frac{n}{2}(1+\log 2\pi\sigma^{2})

be the signal-observation mutual information in the linear model
(
4
) conditional on
\mathbf{X}
, where

\mathsf{P}(\mathbf{y}\mid\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{X})
is the Gaussian likelihood of
\mathbf{y}
and

\mathsf{P}_{g_{*}}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})
is the marginal likelihood
(
6
) under the true prior
g_{*}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\log\mathsf{P}_{g}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})\mid\mathbf{X}]

\displaystyle={-}\DKL(\mathsf{P}_{g_{*}}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})\|\mathsf{P}_{g}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}))+\mathbb{E}[\log\mathsf{P}_{g_{*}}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})\mid\mathbf{X}]

\displaystyle={-}\DKL(\mathsf{P}_{g_{*}}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})\|\mathsf{P}_{g}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}))-I(\mathbf{y},\bm{\theta}^{*})-\frac{n}{2}(1+\log 2\pi\sigma^{2})

(172)

where here and throughout the proof,
\DKL(\cdot)
denotes the
KL-divergence also conditional on
\mathbf{X}
.

Let us denote the inverse noise variance by
s^{-1}=\sigma^{2}
and write

E(s,g)=\mathbb{E}[\YMSE_{*}\mid\mathbf{X}]=n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}\langle\bm{\theta}\rangle-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}\|^{2}\mid\mathbf{X}]

(173)

for the expected
\YMSE_{*}
in the linear model (
4
)
with assumed prior
g
and noise variance

s^{-1}
. We clarify that this means
\langle\cdot\rangle
in
(
173
) is the posterior average under the law

\mathsf{P}_{g}(\bm{\theta}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})\propto\exp\bigg({-}\frac{s}{2}\|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\log g(\theta_{j})\bigg)

and
\mathbb{E}[\cdot\mid\mathbf{X}]
is
the expectation over
(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
where

\bm{\varepsilon}
also has variance
s^{-1}
. We write also

I[s],\DKL[s]
for the above quantities
I(\mathbf{y},\bm{\theta}^{*})
and

\DKL(\mathsf{P}_{g_{*}}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})\|\mathsf{P}_{g}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}))
in this model with noise
variance
s^{-1}
. Then

[
90
, Theorem 2]
and
[
91
, Eq. (24)]
show
the I-MMSE relations

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}I[s]=\frac{n}{2}E(s,g_{*}),\qquad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\DKL[s]=\frac{n}{2}\big(E(s,g)-E(s,g_{*})\big).

For any fixed
n,d
and
\mathbf{X}
,
in the limit
s\to 0
, it is direct to check
that
I[s]\to 0
and
\DKL[s]\to 0
. Thus, for

I(\mathbf{y},\bm{\theta}^{*})\equiv I[\sigma^{-2}]
and

\DKL(\mathsf{P}_{g_{*}}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})\|\mathsf{P}_{g}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}))\equiv\DKL[\sigma^{-2}]
in the original model with noise variance
\sigma^{2}
,
integrating these I-MMSE relations shows

\DKL(\mathsf{P}_{g_{*}}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})\|\mathsf{P}_{g}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}))+I(\mathbf{y},\bm{\theta}^{*})=\frac{n}{2}\int_{0}^{\sigma^{-2}}E(s,g)\mathrm{d}s.

(174)

Assumption
2.7
(b) ensures that the posterior LSI (
46
)
holds a.s. in the
model with any noise variance
s^{-1}\in[\sigma^{2},\infty)
. Then
applying Corollary
2.10
(a) already shown and the
concentration of
\YMSE_{*}
in Proposition
4.11
,
we have
E(s,g)\to\ymse_{*}(s,g)
a.s. for each
s^{-1}\in(\sigma^{2},\infty)
,
where
\ymse_{*}(s,g)
is defined by (
42
) via the DMFT limit of the
Langevin dynamics (
7
) with fixed prior
g(\cdot)
in
the linear model with noise variance
s^{-1}
. To apply dominated convergence,
we note that on the event
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
,
by the extension (
110
) of (
106
), we have
E(s,g)\leq C
for a constant
C>0

uniformly over all
s\in[0,\sigma^{-2}]
and all
n,d
.
Then, since
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
holds a.s. for all large
n,d
,
taking the limit
n,d\to\infty
and
applying the bounded convergence theorem to (
174
)
shows that almost surely,

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\Big(\DKL(\mathsf{P}_{g_{*}}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X})\|\mathsf{P}_{g}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}))+I(\mathbf{y},\bm{\theta}^{*})\Big)=\frac{\delta}{2}\int_{0}^{\sigma^{-2}}\ymse_{*}(s,g)\mathrm{d}s.

(175)

Let us now fix the assumed prior
g(\cdot)
, write
\ymse_{*}(s)\equiv\ymse_{*}(s,g)
, and
let
(\mse(s),\mse_{*}(s),\omega(s),\omega_{*}(s))
denote the fixed points
(
43
) corresponding to
\ymse_{*}(s)
.
Recall the marginal density
\mathsf{P}_{g,\omega}(y)
of the
scalar channel model (
39
), and define

\displaystyle f(\omega,\omega_{*},s)={-}\mathbb{E}_{g_{*},\omega_{*}}\log\mathsf{P}_{g,\omega}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\left(2\delta+\log\frac{2\pi}{\omega}-\delta\log\frac{\delta s}{\omega}+(1-\delta)\frac{\omega}{\omega_{*}}+\frac{\omega}{s}\Big(\frac{\omega}{\omega_{*}}-2\Big)\right)

(176)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\omega}{2}\mathbb{E}\,\theta^{*2}-\mathbb{E}\log\int\exp\Big(\omega\theta(\theta^{*}+\omega_{*}^{-1/2}z)-\frac{\omega}{2}\theta^{2}\Big)g(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta}_{:=\mathrm{I}}-\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\Big(2\delta-\delta\log\frac{\delta s}{\omega}-\frac{\delta\omega}{\omega_{*}}+\frac{\omega}{s}\Big(\frac{\omega}{\omega_{*}}-2\Big)\Big)}_{:=\mathrm{II}}.

Here, the expectations in the second line are over

\theta^{*}\sim g_{*}
and
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, and we have
applied the explicit form of
\mathsf{P}_{g,\omega}(y)
and evaluated

\mathbb{E}_{g_{*},\omega_{*}}
under the true model
y=\theta^{*}+\omega_{*}^{-1/2}z
with
some some algebraic simplification. We now claim that

\frac{\delta}{2}\int_{0}^{s}\ymse_{*}(t)\mathrm{d}t=f(\omega(s),\omega_{*}(s),s)

(177)

for all
s\in(0,\sigma^{-2})
. To show this, it suffices to check

\lim_{s\to 0}f(\omega(s),\omega_{*}(s),s)=0
and
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}f(\omega(s),\omega_{*}(s),s)=\frac{\delta}{2}\ymse_{*}(s)
,
which we may do as follows:

•

Let
\MSE(s),\MSE_{*}(s)
denote the values of
\MSE,\MSE_{*}
in a linear
model with noise variance
s^{-1}
. On the event
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
, the
bound (
110
) implies that
\MSE(s),\MSE_{*}(s)\leq C(1+s\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}/d)
for a constant
C>0
(independent of
s
)
and for all
s^{-1}\in(\sigma^{2},\infty)
.
Taking the almost sure limit as
n,d\to\infty
shows that
\mse(s),\mse_{*}(s)\leq C
. In particular, in the limit
s\to 0
, we have that

\mse(s),\mse_{*}(s)
remain bounded, so

\omega(s),\omega_{*}(s)\sim\delta s
by the fixed point relation
(
43
). Then

\omega(s)\to 0
,
\omega_{*}(s)\to 0
,
\omega(s)/s\to\delta
, and

\omega(s)/\omega_{*}(s)\to 1
as
s\to 0
. Applying this to (
176
)
shows

\lim_{s\to 0}f(\omega(s),\omega_{*}(s),s)=0.

•

Differentiating the term
\mathrm{I}
of (
176
)
in
\omega,\omega_{*}
and applying
Gaussian integration-by-parts with respect to
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, we may check
that

\displaystyle\partial_{\omega}\mathrm{I}
