\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{0},\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}
, it is direct to check that almost surely,

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,s)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\langle{\bm{\theta}^{t}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{s}\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\Tr\bigg(\mathbb{E}(\theta^{0})^{2}\cdot e^{-\bm{\Lambda}(t+s)}+\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}\cdot\Big(\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t})\bm{\Lambda}^{-2}(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}s})\Big(\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\int_{0}^{t\wedge s}2e^{-\bm{\Lambda}(t+s-2r)}\mathrm{d}r\bigg)+\frac{1}{d}\Tr\bigg(\mathbb{E}\varepsilon^{2}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{X}}{\sigma^{2}}(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t})\bm{\Lambda}^{-2}(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}s})\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}}{\sigma^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int\bigg[\mathbb{E}(\theta^{0})^{2}\cdot e^{-(\lambda+x)(t+s)}+\frac{\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}x^{2}+x}{(\lambda+x)^{2}}(1-e^{-(\lambda+x)t})(1-e^{-(\lambda+x)s})

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\frac{1}{\lambda+x}\Big(e^{-(\lambda+x)|t-s|}-e^{-(\lambda+x)(t+s)}\Big)\bigg]\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,*)=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\langle{\bm{\theta}^{t}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\Tr\Big(\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}\bm{\Lambda}^{-1}(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t})\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}x}{\lambda+x}(1-e^{-(\lambda+x)t})\mu(\mathrm{d}x).

Furthermore, the above form (
228
) for
\bm{\theta}^{t}
implies

P_{t}(\bm{\theta})=e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t}\bm{\theta}++\bm{\Lambda}^{-1}\Big(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t}\Big)\Big(\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}}{\sigma^{2}}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\varepsilon}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big).

(229)

Then
\nabla P_{t}e_{j}(\bm{\theta})=\nabla[\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}P_{t}(\bm{\theta})]=e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t}\mathbf{e}_{j}
is a constant function not depending on

\bm{\theta}
, and

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}[\nabla e_{j}^{\top}\nabla P_{t-s}e_{j}](\bm{\theta}^{s})=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\Tr e^{-\bm{\Lambda}(t-s)}=\int e^{-(\lambda+x)(t-s)}\mu(\mathrm{d}x).

By Theorem
4.3
,
this shows the forms of
C_{\theta}(t,s)
,
C_{\theta}(t,*)
, and
R_{\theta}(t,s)
.

From (
228
) and (
229
), we have also

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{t}-\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{X}e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t}\bm{\theta}^{0}+\mathbf{X}\Big(\bm{\Lambda}^{-1}\Big(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t}\Big)\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}}{\sigma^{2}}-\mathbf{I}\Big)\bm{\theta}^{*}+\Big(\mathbf{X}\bm{\Lambda}^{-1}\Big(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t}\Big)\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}}{\sigma^{2}}-\mathbf{I}_{n}\Big)\bm{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{X}e^{-\bm{\Lambda}(t-s)}\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}

and
\nabla P_{t}x_{i}(\bm{\theta})=(\sqrt{\delta}/\sigma)\nabla[\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}P_{t}(\bm{\theta})]=(\sqrt{\delta}/\sigma)e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}
. Then

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\eta}(t,s)=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{t}-\mathbf{y})^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s}-\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d\sigma^{2}}\Tr\bigg(\mathbb{E}(\theta^{0})^{2}\cdot e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}e^{-\bm{\Lambda}s}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}\cdot\Big(\bm{\Lambda}^{-1}\Big(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t}\Big)\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}}{\sigma^{2}}-\mathbf{I}\Big)\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\Big(\bm{\Lambda}^{-1}\Big(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}s}\Big)\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}}{\sigma^{2}}-\mathbf{I}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\int_{0}^{t\wedge s}2\,e^{-\bm{\Lambda}(t-r)}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}e^{-\bm{\Lambda}(s-r)}\mathrm{d}r\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt+\frac{1}{d\sigma^{2}}\Tr\bigg(\mathbb{E}\varepsilon^{2}\cdot\Big(\mathbf{X}\bm{\Lambda}^{-1}\Big(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}t}\Big)\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}}{\sigma^{2}}-\mathbf{I}_{n}\Big)^{\top}\Big(\mathbf{X}\bm{\Lambda}^{-1}\Big(\mathbf{I}-e^{-\bm{\Lambda}s}\Big)\frac{\mathbf{X}^{\top}}{\sigma^{2}}-\mathbf{I}_{n}\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int\bigg[\mathbb{E}(\theta^{0})^{2}xe^{-(\lambda+x)(t+s)}+(\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}x+1)\Big(\frac{x}{\lambda+x}(1-e^{-(\lambda+x)t})-1\Big)\Big(\frac{x}{\lambda+x}(1-e^{-(\lambda+x)s})-1\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+(\delta-1)+\frac{x}{\lambda+x}\Big(e^{-(\lambda+x)|t-s|}-e^{-(\lambda+x)(t+s)}\Big)\bigg]\mu(\mathrm{d}x),

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(t,s)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\nabla x_{i}^{\top}\nabla P_{t-s}x_{i}](\bm{\theta}^{s})=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\sigma^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}e^{-\bm{\Lambda}(t-s)}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d\sigma^{2}}\Tr\mathbf{X}e^{-\bm{\Lambda}(t-s)}\mathbf{X}^{\top}=\int xe^{-(\lambda+x)(t-s)}\mu(\mathrm{d}x).

By Theorem
4.3
, this shows the forms of
C_{\eta}(t,s)

and
R_{\eta}(t,s)
.
∎

From Lemma
B.1
it is apparent that the approximations
(
30
–
32
) and
(
34
–
35
) hold with

\varepsilon(t)=Ce^{-ct}
and

\begin{gathered}c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{init}}(s)={-}\int\frac{\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}x^{2}+x}{(\lambda+x)^{2}}e^{-(\lambda+x)s}\mu(\mathrm{d}x),\qquad c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)=\int\frac{\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}x^{2}+x}{(\lambda+x)^{2}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\\
c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)=c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)+\int\frac{1}{\lambda+x}e^{-(\lambda+x)\tau}\mu(\mathrm{d}x),\qquad r_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)=\int e^{-(\lambda+x)\tau}\mu(\mathrm{d}x),\qquad c_{\theta}(*)=\int\frac{\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}x}{\lambda+x}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\\
c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{init}}(s)=\int\frac{(\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}x+1)\lambda x}{(\lambda+x)^{2}}e^{-(\lambda+x)s}\mu(\mathrm{d}x),\qquad c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)=\int\frac{(\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}x+1)\lambda^{2}}{(\lambda+x)^{2}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)+\delta-1\\
c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)=c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\infty)+\int\frac{x}{\lambda+x}e^{-(\lambda+x)\tau}\mu(\mathrm{d}x),\qquad r_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}(\tau)=\int xe^{-(\lambda+x)\tau}\mu(\mathrm{d}x).\end{gathered}

These functions
c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}},c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}
have the forms
(
33
) for the positive, finite measures

\mu_{\theta}(\mathrm{d}a)=a^{-1}\mu(\mathrm{d}(a-\lambda))

and
\mu_{\eta}(\mathrm{d}a)=[(a-\lambda)/a]\mu(\mathrm{d}(a-\lambda))
supported on

a\in[\lambda,\infty)
. Furthermore, these functions

c_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}},c_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}},r_{\theta}^{\mathrm{tti}},r_{\eta}^{\mathrm{tti}}
satisfy
the fluctuation-dissipation relations (
36
), verifying all
conditions of Definition
2.4
.

Appendix C
Sufficient conditions for a log-Sobolev inequality

We prove Proposition
2.8
on a log-Sobolev inequality for the
posterior law.

Lemma C.1
.

Under Assumption
2.2
(a), the prior density
g(\cdot)
satisfies
the LSI (
17
). Furthermore, consider the law

\mathsf{P}(\theta)=\frac{g(\theta)e^{{-}\frac{a}{2}\theta^{2}+b\theta}}{Z},\qquad Z=\int g(\theta)e^{{-}\frac{a}{2}\theta^{2}+b\theta}\mathrm{d}\theta.

(230)

For any
a>0
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
, this law
\mathsf{P}(\theta)
also satisfies the LSI
(
17
). Both statements hold with the
constant
C_{\mathrm{LSI}}=(4/c_{0})\exp(8r_{0}^{2}(c_{0}+C)^{2}/(\pi c_{0}))
where
C,c_{0},r_{0}
are
the constants of Assumption
2.2
(a).

Proof.

Applying
x=\min(x,{-c_{0}})+\max(x+c_{0},0)
, define

\displaystyle\ell_{-}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\log g(0)+(\log g)^{\prime}(0)\cdot\theta+\int_{0}^{\theta}\int_{0}^{x}\min\Big((\log g)^{\prime\prime}(u),-c_{0}\Big)\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\ell_{+}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\theta}\int_{0}^{x}\max\Big((\log g)^{\prime\prime}(u)+c_{0},0\Big)\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}x

so that
\log g(\theta)=\ell_{-}(\theta)+\ell_{+}(\theta)
. Then set

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}_{-}(\theta)

\displaystyle={-}\log Z-a\theta^{2}+b\theta+\ell_{-}(\theta)

so that
\log\mathsf{P}(\theta)=\tilde{\ell}_{-}(\theta)+\ell_{+}(\theta)
.
By definition we have

\ell_{-}^{\prime\prime}(\theta)=\min((\log g)^{\prime\prime}(\theta),-c_{0})\leq-c_{0}
and also

\tilde{\ell}_{-}^{\prime\prime}(\theta)=-a+\ell_{-}^{\prime\prime}(\theta)\leq-c_{0}
.
We have
(\log g)^{\prime\prime}(u)+c_{0}\leq 0

for all
|u|>r_{0}
and
(\log g)^{\prime\prime}(u)+c_{0}\leq c_{0}+C
for all
|u|\leq r_{0}
.
Hence
|\ell_{+}^{\prime}(\theta)|\leq r_{0}(c_{0}+C)
. Thus both
\log g(\theta)
and

\log\mathsf{P}(\theta)
are sums of a
c_{0}
-strongly-log-concave
potential
\ell_{-}(\theta)
or
\tilde{\ell}_{-}(\theta)
and a

r_{0}(c_{0}+C)
-Lipschitz perturbation
\ell_{+}(\theta)
. Then

[
95
, Lemma 2.1]
shows that both laws satisfy a LSI
with constant
C_{\mathrm{LSI}}=(4/c_{0})\exp(8r_{0}^{2}(c_{0}+C)^{2}/(\pi c_{0}))
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.8
.

Under condition (a), the posterior density is strongly
log-concave, satisfying

{-}\nabla^{2}\log\mathsf{P}_{g}(\bm{\theta}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\diag\Big((\log g)^{\prime\prime}(\theta_{j})\Big)_{j=1}^{d}\succeq c_{0}\mathbf{I}.

Hence (
46
) with
C_{\mathrm{LSI}}=2/c_{0}
follows from the Bakry-Emery
criterion. Clearly this holds for any noise variance
\sigma^{2}>0
,
verifying Assumption
2.7
.

The proof under condition (b) is an adaptation of the argument of

[
96
]
; see also
[
10
, Theorem 3.4]

and
[
97
]
for similar specializations to
the linear model. Under the conditions for
\mathbf{X}
of
Assumption
2.1
, by the Bai-Yin law
(
[
98
, Theorem 3.1]
), for any
\varepsilon>0
the event

\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{X})=\Big\{(\sqrt{\delta}-1)_{+}^{2}-\varepsilon\leq\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X})\leq(\sqrt{\delta}+1)^{2}+\varepsilon\Big\}

hold a.s. for all large
n,d
(where
\delta=\lim n/d
). Thus, choosing some
sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0
and setting

\kappa=(\sqrt{\delta}-1)_{+}^{2}-2\varepsilon,\quad\tau^{2}=\sigma^{2}\Big([(\sqrt{\delta}+1)^{2}-(\sqrt{\delta}-1)_{+}^{2}+3\varepsilon]^{-1}-\varepsilon\Big),\quad\bm{\Sigma}=\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\kappa\,\mathbf{I})^{-1}-\tau^{2}\,\mathbf{I},

we have
\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\kappa\,\mathbf{I}\succeq\varepsilon\,\mathbf{I}
,

(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\kappa\,\mathbf{I})^{-1}\succeq(\frac{\tau^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+\varepsilon)\,\mathbf{I}
, and
hence
\bm{\Sigma}\succeq\varepsilon\sigma^{2}
on
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{X})
.
Since
\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\kappa\,\mathbf{I})^{-1}=\bm{\Sigma}+\tau^{2}\mathbf{I}
,
we have the Gaussian convolution identity

e^{-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\bm{\theta}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\kappa\mathbf{I})\bm{\theta}}\propto\int e^{-\frac{1}{2\tau^{2}}\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\varphi}\|_{2}^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\bm{\varphi}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{\varphi}}\mathrm{d}\bm{\varphi}.

Then the posterior density
\mathsf{P}_{g}(\bm{\theta}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})

satisfies

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{g}(\bm{\theta}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle\propto\exp\Big({-}\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}\|^{2}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{d}g(\theta_{j})

\displaystyle\propto\exp\Big({-}\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\bm{\theta}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\kappa\mathbf{I})\bm{\theta}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{d}g(\theta_{j})\exp\Big({-}\frac{\kappa}{2\sigma^{2}}\theta_{j}^{2}+\frac{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{y}}{\sigma^{2}}\theta_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\propto\int e^{-\frac{1}{2}\bm{\varphi}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{\varphi}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}g(\theta_{j})\exp\Big({-}\frac{\kappa}{2\sigma^{2}}\theta_{j}^{2}-\frac{1}{2\tau^{2}}(\theta_{j}-\varphi_{j})^{2}+\frac{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{y}}{\sigma^{2}}\theta_{j}\Big)\mathrm{d}\bm{\varphi}.

Defining

\displaystyle q_{\varphi_{j}}(\theta_{j})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{j}(\varphi_{j})}g(\theta_{j})\exp\Big({-}\frac{\kappa}{2\sigma^{2}}\theta_{j}^{2}-\frac{1}{2\tau^{2}}(\theta_{j}-\varphi_{j})^{2}+\frac{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{y}}{\sigma^{2}}\theta_{j}\Big),

\displaystyle Z_{j}(\varphi_{j})

\displaystyle=\int g(\theta_{j})\exp\Big({-}\frac{\kappa}{2\sigma^{2}}\theta_{j}^{2}-\frac{1}{2\tau^{2}}(\theta_{j}-\varphi_{j})^{2}+\frac{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{y}}{\sigma^{2}}\theta_{j}\Big)\mathrm{d}\theta_{j},

\displaystyle\mu(\bm{\varphi})

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\bm{\varphi}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{\varphi}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}Z_{j}(\varphi_{j})}{\int e^{-\frac{1}{2}{\bm{\varphi}^{\prime}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{\varphi}^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{d}Z_{j}(\varphi_{j}^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\bm{\varphi}^{\prime}}

this gives the mixture-of-products representation

\mathsf{P}_{g}(\bm{\theta}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})=\int\underbrace{\prod_{j=1}^{d}q_{\varphi_{j}}(\theta_{j})}_{:=q_{\bm{\varphi}}(\bm{\theta})}\mu(\bm{\varphi})\mathrm{d}\bm{\varphi}.

Then for any
f\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\Ent[f(\bm{\theta})^{2}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y}]=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\varphi}\sim\mu}\Ent_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2}+\Ent_{\bm{\varphi}\sim\mu}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2}.

(231)

For the first term of (
231
),
note that inside the exponential defining
q_{\varphi_{j}}(\theta_{j})
we have

\kappa\geq-2\varepsilon
and
\tau^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}((1+3\varepsilon)^{-1}-\varepsilon)
, so
the coefficient of
\theta_{j}^{2}
is negative for sufficiently
small
\varepsilon>0
. Then
by Lemma
C.1
,
q_{\varphi_{j}}(\theta_{j})
satisfies
the univariate LSI (
17
) with constant

C_{\mathrm{LSI}}:=(4/c_{0})\exp(8r_{0}^{2}(c_{0}+C)^{2}/(\pi c_{0}))
. So the product law

q_{\bm{\varphi}}
satisfies the LSI with the same constant by tensorization, and

\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\varphi}\sim\mu}\Ent_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{LSI}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\varphi}\sim\mu}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}\|\nabla f(\bm{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}=C_{\mathrm{LSI}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q}\|\nabla f(\bm{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}.

(232)

For the second term of (
231
), note that

\displaystyle{-}\nabla_{\bm{\varphi}}^{2}\log\mu(\bm{\varphi})=\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\diag\Big((\log Z_{j})^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{j})\Big)_{j=1}^{d}=\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}+\diag\Big(\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}-\frac{1}{\tau^{4}}\Var_{\theta_{j}\sim q_{\varphi_{j}}}[\theta_{j}]\Big)_{j=1}^{d}.

The LSI for
q_{\varphi_{j}}
implies

\Var_{\theta_{j}\sim q_{\varphi_{j}}}[\theta_{j}]\leq(C_{\mathrm{LSI}}/2)
by its implied
Poincaré inequality. Applying

(\sqrt{\delta}+1)^{2}-(\sqrt{\delta}-1)_{+}^{2}=4\sqrt{\delta}\mathbf{1}\{\delta>1\}+(\sqrt{\delta}+1)^{2}\mathbf{1}\{\delta\leq 1\}

and the given condition (b) for
\sigma^{2}
,
we see that for a sufficiently small choice of
\varepsilon>0
,
we have
\tau^{2}\geq C_{\mathrm{LSI}}/[2(1-\varepsilon)]
. Then this gives

{-}\nabla_{\bm{\varphi}}^{2}\log\mu(\bm{\varphi})\succeq(\varepsilon/\tau^{2})\mathbf{I}
.
Then by the Bakry-Emery criterion,
\mu
satisfies the LSI
\Ent_{\bm{\varphi}\sim\mu}f(\bm{\varphi})^{2}\leq(2\tau^{2}/\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\varphi}\sim\mu}\|\nabla f(\bm{\varphi})\|_{2}^{2}
, hence

\Ent_{\bm{\varphi}\sim\mu}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2}\leq\frac{2\tau^{2}}{\varepsilon}\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\varphi}\sim\mu}\|\nabla_{\bm{\varphi}}(\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2})^{1/2}\|_{2}^{2}.

Denote by
q_{\bm{\varphi}^{-j}}
the product of components of
q_{\bm{\varphi}}

other than the
j^{\text{th}}
. We compute

\partial_{\varphi_{j}}(\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2})^{1/2}=\frac{\partial_{\varphi_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2}}{2(\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2})^{1/2}}=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}^{-j}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}^{-j}}}\partial_{\varphi_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{j}\sim q_{\varphi_{j}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2}}{2(\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2})^{1/2}}=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}^{-j}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}^{-j}}}\Cov_{\theta_{j}\sim q_{\varphi_{j}}}[f(\bm{\theta})^{2},\theta_{j}]}{2\tau^{2}(\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2})^{1/2}}.

We apply
[
99
, Proposition 2.2]
:
For any law
\nu
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying a LSI
\Ent_{\nu}f^{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{LSI}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\|\nabla f\|_{2}^{2}
, and for any smooth functions
f,g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\Cov_{\nu}[f^{2},g]\leq C\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\nabla g(\bm{\theta})\|_{2}\cdot(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}f^{2})^{1/2}(\mathbb{E}_{\nu}\|\nabla f\|_{2}^{2})^{1/2}

where
C
depends only on the LSI constant
C_{\mathrm{LSI}}
of
\nu
.
Applying this to the univariate law
\nu=q_{\varphi_{j}}
, followed by
Cauchy-Schwarz,

\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}^{-j}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}^{-j}}}\Cov_{\theta_{j}\sim q_{\varphi_{j}}}[f(\bm{\theta})^{2},\theta_{j}]\leq C_{1}(\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2})^{1/2}(\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}(\partial_{\theta_{j}}f(\bm{\theta}))^{2})^{1/2}

where
C_{1}
depends only on the LSI constant
C_{\mathrm{LSI}}
for
q_{\varphi_{j}}
.
Summing over
j=1,\ldots,d
gives

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\bm{\varphi}}(\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2})^{1/2}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\big[\partial_{\varphi_{j}}(\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2}))^{1/2}\big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Big(\frac{C_{1}}{2\tau^{2}}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}(\partial_{\theta_{j}}f(\bm{\theta}))^{2}=\Big(\frac{C_{1}}{2\tau^{2}}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}\|\nabla f(\bm{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}.

Thus

\Ent_{\bm{\varphi}\sim\mu}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}f(\bm{\theta})^{2}\leq\frac{2\tau^{2}}{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{C_{1}}{2\tau^{2}}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim\mu}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}\sim q_{\bm{\varphi}}}\|\nabla f(\bm{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{2\varepsilon\tau^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla f(\bm{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y}].

(233)

Applying (
232
) and (
233
) to
(
231
) completes the proof of (
46
), on the above event

\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{X})
. Since the given
condition (b) also holds for all
\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\geq\sigma^{2}
when it holds
for
\sigma^{2}
, this verifies Assumption
2.7
.

Finally, under condition (c), we note that on the above event
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{X})
we
have

{-}\nabla^{2}\log\mathsf{P}_{g}(\bm{\theta}\mid\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y})=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\diag\Big((\log g)^{\prime\prime}(\theta_{j})\Big)_{j=1}^{d}\succeq\varepsilon\mathbf{I}

for all
\sigma^{2}\leq[(\sqrt{\delta}-1)^{2}-\varepsilon]/(C+\varepsilon)
,
so (
46
) holds by the Bakry-Emery criterion. Choosing
\varepsilon>0

small enough, under condition (c) we have

[(\sqrt{\delta}-1)^{2}-\varepsilon]/(C+\varepsilon)>4C_{0}\sqrt{\delta}
, so that
(
46
) holds with
C_{\mathrm{LSI}}=2/\varepsilon
for

\sigma^{2}>[(\sqrt{\delta}-1)^{2}-\varepsilon]/(C+\varepsilon)
by the analysis of condition (b).
Thus, on
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{X})
, (
46
) holds with a uniform constant
C_{\mathrm{LSI}}>0

for all
\sigma^{2}>0
, again verifying Assumption
2.7
.
∎

Appendix D
Auxiliary lemmas

Lemma D.1

(Coupling of Gaussian processes)
.

Let
\{K(t,s)\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
and
\{\tilde{K}(t,s)\}_{t,s\in[0,T]}
be two
positive semidefinite covariance kernels such that for some
\varepsilon>0
and
C_{0}>0

\displaystyle\sup_{t,s\in[0,T]}|K(t,s)-\tilde{K}(t,s)|\leq\varepsilon,

(234)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{t,s\in[0,T]}K(t,t)+K(s,s)-2K(t,s)\leq C_{0}|t-s|.

(235)

Then there exists a coupling of the two mean-zero
Gaussian processes
\{u^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
and

\{\tilde{u}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
with covariances
\mathbb{E}[u^{t}u^{s}]=K(t,s)
and

\mathbb{E}[\tilde{u}^{t}\tilde{u}^{s}]=\tilde{K}(t,s)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[(u^{t}-\tilde{u}^{t})^{2}]\leq(6C_{0}+3)\sqrt{T\varepsilon}+15\varepsilon.

Proof.

Fix
\gamma>0
, and let
\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor=\max\{i\gamma:i\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},i\gamma\leq t\}
where
\mathbb{Z}_{+}=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
. Let
v^{t}=u^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}

and
\tilde{v}^{t}=\tilde{u}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}
so that

\mathbb{E}[v^{t}v^{s}]=K(\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor,\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor)
and

\mathbb{E}[\tilde{v}^{t}\tilde{v}^{s}]=\tilde{K}(\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor,\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor)
.
Then by (
235
) and (
234
),

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}(u^{t}-v^{t})^{2}\leq C_{0}\gamma,\qquad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{u}^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})^{2}\leq C_{0}\gamma+4\varepsilon.

Let
X=(v^{0},v^{\gamma},v^{2\gamma},\ldots,v^{\left\lfloor T\right\rfloor})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
, where here

N\leq T/\gamma+1
, and similarly let

\tilde{X}=(\tilde{v}^{0},\tilde{v}^{\gamma},\tilde{v}^{2\gamma},\ldots,\tilde{v}^{\left\lfloor T\right\rfloor})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
. Let
\Sigma,\tilde{\Sigma}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
be the
covariance matrices of
X,\tilde{X}
, so

\Sigma_{ij}=K(i\gamma,j\gamma)
and

\tilde{\Sigma}_{ij}=\tilde{K}(i\gamma,j\gamma)
. Coupling
X
and
\tilde{X}

by
X=\Sigma^{1/2}Z
and
\tilde{X}=\tilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}Z
where
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I)
,
for each
i=1,\ldots,N
,

\mathbb{E}(X_{i}-\tilde{X}_{i})^{2}=\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}(\Sigma^{1/2}-\tilde{\Sigma}^{1/2})^{2}\mathbf{e}_{i}\leq\|\Sigma^{1/2}-\tilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{\leq}}\|\Sigma-\tilde{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq N\varepsilon.

Here
(*)
follows from
[
100
, Theorem X.1.3]
,
and the last inequality applies (
234
). Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}(u^{t}-\tilde{u}^{t})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}(u^{t}-v^{t})^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}(v^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{u}^{t}-\tilde{v}^{t})^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 6C_{0}\gamma+12\varepsilon+3\max_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(X_{i}-\tilde{X}_{i})^{2}\leq 6C_{0}\gamma+12\varepsilon+3(T/\gamma+1)\varepsilon.

The conclusion follows by choosing
\gamma=\sqrt{T\varepsilon}
.
∎
